# PRODUCTION AUDIT — KoruBeni (com.poyrazoncel.korubeni)

> Audit of every individual checkbox in [`docs/MASTER_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`](docs/MASTER_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md).

## Evidence provenance

> **The single stamp this document used to carry named an OLDER tree than the one it
> graded.** `INDEPENDENT_REVIEW_ROUND_2.md` R2-08 showed 9,972 insertions across 42 files
> between the stamped commit and the tree being assessed, including the safety-critical
> files whose rows were being written. A reader could not tell which rows described which
> tree, which is the only property a stamp exists to provide. Three separate facts are
> therefore recorded separately, and they are not interchangeable.

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| **VERIFIED CODE REVISION** — the tree every `TEST`/`CMD`/`RUN` result below was produced against | `273864f` (branch `feat/tutundurma-ucretsiz-prova`) |
| **Documentation revision** — the commit that carries this file's current text | the commit containing this line; it changes no `lib/`, `android/` or `test/` source |
| **Worktree at verification time** | clean (`git status --porcelain` empty); the secret scan refuses to run otherwise |
| Verification date | 2026-08-13 |

> Rows dated **2026-08-12** were produced against the earlier `fe83771` tree and carry
> their own date in the evidence cell. Where a row was re-verified against `273864f` its
> evidence says so explicitly. Evidence from one revision is never presented as proof of
> behaviour introduced in another.



**Scope note.** KoruBeni is an Android-only, offline-first Flutter personal-safety app with **no developer backend, no accounts, no analytics/telemetry, and no AI**. A large share of this checklist targets server/web/AI products; those requirements are marked `N/A` with a per-item justification, never for difficulty. Where a requirement genuinely applies but could not be verified with available evidence it is marked `UNVERIFIED`, and where an external dependency blocks it, `BLOCKED`.


## Verification method codes

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `RUN` | Live app interaction — Android emulator `Medium_Phone_API_36.1` (API 36), `app-play-debug`, full first-run walkthrough with screenshots, 2026-08-12 |
| `TEST` | Automated test evidence (`flutter test`, Kotlin unit, Android instrumentation) |
| `CMD` | Command output captured during this audit |
| `SRC` | Concrete source/config implementation where runtime verification does not apply |
| `DOC` | Version-controlled repository document |
| `NONE` | No verification possible with available evidence |

## Baseline commands run

> Recorded as the **exact invocations**, because the previous table's command lines could
> not execute as written -- `scan_release_secrets.py` and
> `verify_release_change_classification.py` both have required arguments, and
> `audit_dependencies_osv.sh` requires `--output` (R2-10). Measured against the verified
> code revision above.

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `flutter analyze --no-fatal-infos` | **No issues found!** (2.2s) |
| `flutter test --no-pub` | **1139 passed / 0 failed** |
| `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` | **CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS** -- all 5 critical safety files >=90% |
| `bash scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh --output <path>` | **OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS** -- 197 pub + 203 maven queries, `findingCount: 0` |
| `python3 scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean --output <path>` | **RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS** -- 733 text + 46 binary files, 0 findings |
| `python3 scripts/verify_release_change_classification.py --config config/release_change_classification.json --output <path>` | **RELEASE_CHANGE_CLASSIFICATION_PASS** |
| `python3 scripts/verify_audit_accounting.py` | **AUDIT_ACCOUNTING_PASS** -- checklist=1738 audit=1738 missing=0 duplicated=0 unaccounted=0 sections=80 launchMatrix=24 |
| `dart format --output=none --set-exit-if-changed lib/ test/` | **63 of 388 files would change** -- no format gate in CI (deliberate, see MP-27-003) |
| `flutter build apk --debug --flavor play --target-platform android-arm64` | **Built successfully** (`app-play-debug.apk`), 2026-08-12 against `fe83771` |
| App runtime | **Launched and driven on emulator**, 2026-08-12 against `fe83771` -- consent gate -> onboarding -> contact validation -> PIN setup -> permission rationale/denial -> home -> settings |

| `MP-01-016` | Deep link doğru ekrana götürüyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED. Deep links were ADDED by MP-26-008, so this row is no longer N/A -- its own remediation said 'Becomes Applicable if deep links are added'. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/flows.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/flows.json`, property `measurements.entryPoints.deepLinks`: scheme = korubeni, 7 destinationSlugs, parserIsPure = true. `korubeni://open/<destination>` reaches exactly one allowlisted surface and nothing else; every destination round-trips through its own builder and parser. `test/core/navigation/deep_link_test.dart` (34 cases). | None. The manifest declares a VIEW filter for the korubeni scheme only; an unknown destination, an unknown parameter, a foreign host or a path deeper than one segment is refused with a named reason rather than silently ignored. | None in repo. | `TEST` |
| `MP-01-017` | Unauthorized deep link doğru şekilde login'e yönlendiriyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED. This app has no login; its authentication gate is the local PIN (CLAUDE.md rule 2 forbids biometrics), so the requirement reads as 'an unauthorized deep link is sent through the auth gate'. It is, and by construction rather than by a check: the link layer cannot navigate at all -- it PARKS a validated destination, and the park is consumed only by MainNavigation, which SplashScreen builds after consent, onboarding and the PIN gate. Verifier property `measurements.entryPoints.deepLinks.parkConsumers` = exactly one file. `test/core/navigation/deep_link_test.dart` asserts the single consumer, and that MainNavigation is the else-branch of the PIN gate. | None. | None in repo. | `TEST` |
| `MP-01-018` | Login sonrası kullanıcı orijinal deep link'e dönebiliyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED. The parked destination survives the gate and is delivered afterwards: `PendingDestinationService` holds the latest validated destination, and `MainNavigation` consumes it once on its first frame and again on resume, which is where a return from the PIN re-auth lock lands. Verifier property `measurements.entryPoints.deepLinks`: singleConsume = true, so it fires once rather than re-firing on every rebuild. `test/core/navigation/deep_link_test.dart` covers cold start, warm app, background and locked arrival, plus duplicate and rapid bursts collapsing to one destination. | None. | None in repo. | `TEST` |

| `MP-01-020` | Back/forward cache durumları düşünülmüş. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: Android-only Flutter app. No ios/ or web/ directory exists; flutter_launcher_icons and flutter_native_splash both set ios:false/web:false. | `MP-01-021` | Session expire olursa kullanıcının yaptığı iş mümkün olduğunca kaybolmuyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on device, and measuring it FOUND AND FIXED A SECURITY DEFECT that made the requirement untestable. FIRST RUN: with a half-filled form (the Profile > Kisisel Bilgiler name field holding 'Yarim Kalan Metin'), the app was backgrounded for 141 s against a 120 s re-auth threshold -- and NO LOCK SCREEN APPEARED. Root cause: `AppLifecycleState.inactive` fires not only on the way out but again on the way BACK IN, immediately before `resumed`. `emergency_trigger_host` called `AppLifecycleHandler.onPaused()` for `inactive`, so the resume transition overwrote the paused timestamp with the current time; elapsed computed as ~0 and the lock could never fire at ANY duration. `app_privacy_shield.dart` already documented exactly this inactive-vs-paused distinction, so the codebase disagreed with itself. FIXED with a `lifecycleStartsBackgroundClock()` predicate (paused/hidden/detached only) plus earliest-timestamp-wins in `onPaused()`. AFTER THE FIX, SAME SCENARIO: 135 s in the background produced the PIN lock screen ('Uygulama kilidi. PIN ile acin.'), and after unlocking the app returned to Kisisel Bilgiler with the unsubmitted text 'Kilit Testi Metni' still in the field. So the checklist's lock/unlock cycle now genuinely occurs and in-progress input survives it -- the lock is pushed as a route ON TOP of the form, which stays mounted beneath. | RECORDED HONESTLY, not folded into the pass: unsubmitted input does NOT survive genuine PROCESS DEATH. Measured separately -- `am kill` while backgrounded, then relaunch, produced a new pid and the app restarted at onboarding page 1/5 with the half-filled contact form gone. The app has no Flutter state restoration (`RestorationMixin` / `restorationScopeId`). The checklist's scenario is the lock/unlock cycle, which passes; process-death restoration is a larger change and is named here rather than implied. | test/core/services/app_lifecycle_lock_test.dart -- 7 cases pinning the fix, including the defect itself as a named case ('inactive saati BASLATMAZ') and a mutation control proving the old overwrite-every-call behaviour would fail. Device evidence both before and after is in docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-a11y-perf.md. | `RUN` |
| `MP-01-022` | Kullanıcı bir işlemi iki kere tetiklerse duplicate işlem oluşmuyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | R2-02 FIXED. SRC lib/widgets/panic_button.dart guards re-entry with `if (_pointerDown // _isArmed // _countdownOpening) return;` set synchronously in the tap handler, plus a _pressEpoch generation counter. SRC lib/core/widgets/emergency_trigger_host.dart now acquires `_countdownOpen` SYNCHRONOUSLY before the first await and releases it in `finally` -- it used to be written after a ~2.2s entitlement budget, so two quick-access/volume triggers inside that window stacked two countdown routes. TEST test/core/widgets/emergency_trigger_duplicate_guard_test.dart fires the real trigger 2x and 20x inside the parked resolve window and asserts exactly one route push, plus guard release after success and after rejection. MUTATION: the old ordering yields 2 routes for 2 triggers and 20 for 20. | - | - | `TEST` |

| `MP-03-008` | Border kalınlıkları tutarlı. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED specifically for borders rather than inferred from a generic `width:` scan (which would have wrongly swept in container widths). Across lib/ there are 83 `Border.all` / `BorderSide` constructions. 72 of them (86.7%) specify no width at all and therefore take Flutter's 1.0 default. Of the 11 that are explicit: 1.0 (x3), 1.5 (x3), 2.0 (x4) and 4.0 (x1). So the app effectively ships one hairline border weight with a small, deliberate set of heavier variants for emphasis -- four distinct weights across 83 borders. | None. Border weight is the most consistent geometry dimension measured in this section. | The measurement is reproducible from lib/ with the pattern recorded in this row; the widths are palette-independent and carry no token drift risk. | `SRC` |
| `MP-03-009` | Shadow sistemi tutarlı. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | RESOLVED by measuring first, then deriving. INVENTORY: 27 BoxShadow sites in lib/ with FOURTEEN distinct blur radii (20 x6, 8 x4, 6 x4, 24 x2, 12 x2, then 60/40/30/28/18/15/14/10 once each) and five distinct offsets. Grouping by PURPOSE rather than by number collapses to five rungs in lib/core/design_tokens.dart: resting (neutral, blur 6, 0-2), raised (neutral, blur 8, 0-2), overlay (AppColors.shadowOverlay, blur 24, 0-12, for surfaces over map tiles), brandGlow(color) (alpha 0.3, blur 20, 0-8) and tintedCard(color) (alpha 0.08, blur 14, 0-8). Ten sites migrated. DELIBERATELY NOT FLATTENED: seven shadows are ANIMATED -- they compute blur and spread from an animation value each frame (splash logo breath, onboarding icon pulse, panic-button arming ring, siren colour lerp, emergency-call header) -- and a static rung cannot express an interpolation, so collapsing them would delete motion the app has on purpose. Two further near-misses (home_page blur 12 vs 14, safe_walk blur 12 vs 20) are left rendering exactly as they ship rather than nudged for tidiness. | Elevation/shadow is a convention, not a token. | test/core/shadow_token_ratchet_test.dart: asserts the rungs are distinct and ASCEND in both blur and offset (an overlay that sat closer to the surface than a raised card would invert the hierarchy), that the glow reads stronger than the tint, that the token is actually referenced by at least 8 files (a token nobody uses is worse than the convention it replaced), and that every remaining hand-rolled BoxShadow is named in an allow-list WITH its reason -- with a staleness check so an exemption cannot outlive it. | `TEST` |

The logic is consistent and deliberately flat: this app draws depth with its own `BoxShadow` on dark surfaces rather than with Material elevation, so almost everything is elevation 0 and the raised rung is reserved. | None for elevation. The related SHADOW system is a separate matter and is honestly still PARTIAL under MP-03-009. | test/core/design_token_ratchet_test.dart enumerates the elevation scale from the token file. | `SRC` |

## 4. Design System

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/app_theme.dart (233 lines): one ThemeData pair defines colours and component themes. Values are literals inside component themes rather than a named token layer.


**Gap.** There is no design-token layer: no named spacing, radius, elevation, z-index, motion-duration, easing, breakpoint, icon-size or density scale. Widgets consume raw numbers.


**Remediation.** Add lib/core/design_tokens.dart holding the scales and migrate app_theme.dart plus the highest-traffic widgets onto it. This is a maintainability investment, not a user-visible defect.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-04-001` | Design token sistemi var. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/design_tokens.dart, written by MEASURING lib/ rather than by inventing a system: every rung is a value the app already ships, so nothing rendered changed (CLAUDE.md rule 4). ENFORCED by TEST test/core/design_token_ratchet_test.dart, which builds its allowed sets FROM the token file (so test and tokens cannot disagree), counts off-scale values across lib/ and PINS the current count -- the same accepted-debt ratchet pattern as source_file_size_ratchet_test.dart. A big-bang migration of 131 literals was deliberately NOT done: it would churn hundreds of lines and risk the visual regressions rule 4 exists to prevent. MUTATION: changing one radius from 12 to 13 fails the run with the offending file and value named. The system spans spacing, radius, elevation, icon size, z-layer, breakpoints and density; motion already had its own documented scale in lib/core/motion.dart and is deliberately not duplicated (asserted by the test). It is load-bearing rather than decorative: lib/core/app_theme.dart -- the single place that defines shapes and elevations for every themed component -- now reads the tokens, and the test fails if a numeric elevation reappears there. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-04-002` | Color token'ları var. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/app_theme.dart: ColorScheme is defined once per theme and consumed via Theme.of(context). | - | - | `SRC` |

TEST test/core/design_token_ratchet_test.dart asserts each name exists as a declared token AND that panic_button.dart uses AppColors.emergency while readiness_card.dart uses AppColors.success and AppColors.warning -- so a palette change cannot leave one safety surface on the old red. It also pins raw Color(0x...) literals outside the palette at their current 30, with the message naming the offenders, so a new hard-coded safety colour fails the run. DOC docs/design/design_system.md records the convention. | - | - | `TEST` |

TEST test/core/design_token_ratchet_test.dart pins off-scale font sizes at 77 and asserts the weight set exactly -- a stray w300 or a bold/normal alias fails. DOC docs/design/design_system.md. | The scale is named and ratcheted but NOT routed through the theme: the app sets fontSize inline at 335 sites and reads Theme.of(context).textTheme at zero. The scale was therefore rebuilt FROM the census into nine roles named by product purpose (inline / dense / listItem / action / emphasis / dialog / feature / illustration / hero), every rung a size the app already drew, so the migration changes NO PIXELS -- the same shape as the ZLayer / Breakpoints / DensityTokens migrations. 139 of 162 sites now read a role; the remaining 23 are one-offs enumerated in IconSizes.documentedExceptions. VISUAL SIZE vs HIT TARGET is kept separate and asserted: no role reaches 48 dp, and a widget case proves an IconButton's box and glyph are byte-identical before and after the substitution. `test/core/icon_size_migration_test.dart` (11 cases) fails if a NEW off-scale size appears, if a documented exception goes stale, if an exception duplicates a role, or if adoption falls back. The design-token ratchet's icon pin was TIGHTENED 73 -> 23 rather than left high, since a pin far above reality stops catching drift. | None. TypeScale is the remaining unmigrated scale and stays PARTIAL under MP-04-004 on its own merits: 329 inline fontSize sites whose honest fix is a TextTheme, which is a refactor rather than a rename. | None in repo for icons. | `TEST` |

| `MP-05-025` | Turkish İ/i/ı/I case dönüşümleri test edilmiş. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | TEST test/core/services/turkish_casing_test.dart. The hazard is asymmetric and the test states it: a Turkish-locale lowercase turns I into dotless i, so 'XIAOMI' would fold to 'xiaomi' losing its dots and the OEM battery-guide matcher would fall through to the generic guide on exactly the devices whose battery managers kill background work hardest. Dart's toLowerCase is locale-INVARIANT, which is what every casing site in this app depends on -- all of them machine data (build flavour, HTTP headers, RevenueCat key prefix, Build.MANUFACTURER). The test pins that invariance, drives the OEM matcher with 10 upper-case vendor strings plus generic and empty controls, and includes a GUARD that fails if any NEW case-fold appears whose receiver is not on the machine-data allow-list -- because folding user-entered Turkish text is where the tailoring would actually matter. Also records the measured fact that Dart returns a bare i for dotted-I lowercase, not the Unicode default i+U+0307. | - | - | `TEST` |

## 7. Responsive / Adaptive layout

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/main.dart pins portraitUp/portraitDown and AndroidManifest.xml sets PROPERTY_COMPAT_ALLOW_RESTRICTED_RESIZABILITY=true - Google's documented temporary opt-out from Android 16 large-screen resizability. RUN exercised the 1080x2400 phone form factor only.


**Gap.** Tablet, foldable, landscape and split-screen layouts are explicitly deferred by that manifest opt-out; no adaptive-layout work exists.


**Remediation.** The opt-out expires when the app targets API 37. Start the adaptive-layout workstream now: define breakpoints, verify the four highest-traffic screens on tablet and foldable emulators, and record evidence under docs/qa/.

## 8. Bütün component state'leri

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 37


**Section assessment.** RUN exercised default, filled, focused, invalid, valid, disabled and pressed states on the real components (contact form, PIN pad, primary CTAs, locked Pro cards). No exhaustive state matrix or visual-regression suite exists.


**Gap.** There is no systematic component-state matrix and no visual-regression harness, so state coverage is incidental rather than guaranteed.


**Remediation.** Add golden/visual-regression tests for the shared widgets in lib/widgets/ covering the state matrix, or document the matrix as a manual QA sheet under docs/qa/.

Frame source is SurfaceFlinger per-frame present timestamps for the app's own BLAST SurfaceView layer (`dumpsys SurfaceFlinger --latency`), because `dumpsys gfxinfo` only sees HWUI and reported just 7 frames for this app -- Flutter draws its content on its own raster thread, so gfxinfo is the WRONG instrument here and using it would have produced a meaningless pass. Layout/transition animations measured with 3 repeats each: bottom-nav route changes Ana Sayfa->Harita, Harita->Kisiler, Kisiler->Ayarlar, plus the settings list layout. Worst intervals per run were 18.8-19.9ms against a 16.67ms budget (1.13-1.19x) and ZERO intervals reached 1.5x, let alone the 2x that marks a real dropped frame. The p90 rises from ~16.7ms at rest to ~18.3-18.7ms during a transition, reproducibly -- that pattern was investigated rather than averaged away, and it is present-time jitter around a held 60fps, not frame drops. | None measured. Scroll-triggered work measured over 744 frame intervals across repeated runs (4x each): settings scroll, home scroll, home fling, and a map pan (the heaviest screen, flutter_map tiles). Budget 16.67ms. Results: settings scroll 99.6% of intervals within 1.2x budget and map pan 100% within 1.2x; the single worst interval anywhere was 20.01ms. CRITICALLY, ZERO intervals reached 2x budget in any run -- on a 60Hz panel a genuinely dropped frame appears as a ~33ms interval, and there were none. The 17-20ms spread is emulator present-timestamp jitter, not dropped frames; that distinction is why the bands are reported instead of an average. | None measured on the emulator. Physical low-end hardware remains uncovered (section 41/59 real-device matrix). | Re-run the recorded profile-mode capture if list or animation code changes; a real dropped frame would show as a >=2x band entry. | `RUN` |

## 10. UI fiziği

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** The product has no drag-and-drop surface and no custom spring physics; scrolling and gestures use Flutter defaults. The one bespoke gesture is the panic press-and-hold gate (lib/core/utils/panic_hold_gate.dart), which is test-pinned.


**Gap.** Scroll and gesture behaviour is inherited from the framework and was only spot-checked on an emulator; no physical-device feel pass is recorded.


**Remediation.** Fold a scroll/gesture feel check into the existing physical-device QA matrix rather than building instrumentation for framework defaults.

Surface: lib/core/services/scroll_restoration.dart + settings_page.dart + safety_timeline_screen.dart. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/flows.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/flows.json`, property `measurements.interruptionSafety.scrollRestoration`: settingsUsesPixelOffset = true, timelineRegistersAnchor = true, anchorCarriesIdentity = true, anchorCarriesIndex = true, twoPhaseRestore = true, clampsToCurrentExtent = true, degradesWhenUnregistered = true, schedulesItsOwnFrame = true -- all COMPUTED. TWO LISTS, TWO MECHANISMS, and the difference is the finding: the queue's own note said 'add restorationId to the two long scroll views', which is right for settings (fixed content, so a pixel offset IS the answer) and wrong for the timeline, whose rows are PREPENDED (sorted timestamp DESC) so a restored offset lands on a different event than the one the user was reading. The timeline anchor therefore carries the item IDENTITY and restores estimate-then-correct: the capture index gives a first jump that forces the lazy ListView to BUILD the anchored item, then the item's real geometry aligns it exactly. Behaviour: `test/screens/scroll_restoration_test.dart` (17 cases) drives a real ListView.builder with UNEVEN extents, simulates process death with a fresh restorer fed the decoded anchor, prepends five rows, and asserts the restored position followed the EVENT (960) and not the pixel (500). NEGATIVE CONTROL, in the same file: the naive pixel restore is shown to land on a different event, so the machinery is not overhead. Verifier control: unregistering the anchor and breaking the two-phase restore moves flows.py violations 0 -> 10. Bucket transport across REAL process death (adb am kill, PID change, state restored) is already recorded in docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-state-restoration.md; this row adds a new RestorableValue to that same proven bucket. | None. Three defects were found by driving the real list rather than reading the API: writing an unregistered RestorableValue ASSERTS (so a scroll before restoreState, or any use outside a restoration scope, would have thrown on every notification); GlobalObjectKey.currentContext is null for an unbuilt item in a lazy list, so identity alone cannot find the target; and addPostFrameCallback schedules no frame, so the restore silently never ran even though the anchor and the policy were both correct. | None in repo. | `TEST` |

## 12. Accessibility

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 40


**Section assessment.** Targeted accessibility tests exist for the safety-critical surfaces: TEST test/widgets/panic_button_a11y_test.dart, test/screens/countdown_live_region_test.dart, test/screens/timeline_delete_semantics_test.dart. Semantics wrappers appear in 16 files including lib/main.dart. Reduce-motion and 200% text scaling are both honoured and test-pinned.


**Gap.** No screen-reader (TalkBack) pass, no keyboard pass and no automated accessibility-guideline assertions exist. Flutter ships meetsGuideline matchers (androidTapTargetGuideline, textContrastGuideline, labeledTapTargetGuideline) and none are used.


**Remediation.** Add meetsGuideline assertions to the existing widget tests for the primary screens, then run one TalkBack pass on a physical device and record it under docs/qa/.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Sequence: KEYCODE_TAB x5 on the Settings screen moved focus down the list and rendered a visible focus highlight on the 5th row (Pil Optimizasyonu); KEYCODE_ENTER then ACTIVATED that row and navigated to the Battery Optimization screen. Screenshots at each step. So the app is operable without touch: focus moves, is visible, and activates. Note this is an Android touch product with no keyboard as its primary input; the verification exists because a Bluetooth keyboard or an accessibility switch device produces exactly these events. | - | - | `RUN` |

Screenshots at each step. Tab order followed the visual order top-to-bottom (profile card, then the Pro row, then the two Security rows, then Battery), i.e. reading order, with no jumps backwards or into the bottom navigation before the page content was exhausted. | - | - | `RUN` |

Screenshots at each step. The focused row rendered with a distinctly lighter background than its unfocused siblings, visible in the captured screenshot -- Flutter's Material focus highlight, not a custom one, so it follows the theme automatically. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-12-004` | Focus modal'ın arkasına kaçmıyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on device (API 36 emulator, hardware-keyboard key events) with REAL FOCUSED NODE IDs read from the accessibility tree, not screenshots: `adb shell uiautomator dump` exposes this Flutter app's semantics, so every claim below names the node that actually held focus. Empty focus output was never accepted as a pass. With the data-wipe confirm dialog up, TAB x6 cycled ONLY between the two dialog nodes (Iptal, Cihaz Verilerini Temizle) and never reached a background control; the background nodes remained focused=false throughout. DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED while measuring: initial focus was NOT inside the modal -- the only focused node was the root FlutterView and every semantic node read focused=false, reproduced on two independent modals. Fixed by lib/core/widgets/escape_dismissible.dart (Focus autofocus + skipTraversal, so it holds initial focus without adding a Tab stop); after the fix the focused node with a modal open is a View carrying the dialog's own semantic label. | None for this requirement. Focus cannot reach behind a modal. | Covered continuously by test/widgets/escape_dismissible_test.dart (initial focus inside the modal) plus the device walk recorded in docs/audit/. | `RUN` |
| `MP-12-005` | Focus trap gereken yerde var. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on device (API 36 emulator, hardware-keyboard key events) with REAL FOCUSED NODE IDs read from the accessibility tree, not screenshots: `adb shell uiautomator dump` exposes this Flutter app's semantics, so every claim below names the node that actually held focus. Empty focus output was never accepted as a pass. Forward containment: TAB x6 cycled Iptal -> Cihaz Verilerini Temizle -> Iptal ... indefinitely, never leaving the dialog. Reverse containment: SHIFT+TAB x5 (sent with `adb shell input keycombination KEYCODE_SHIFT_LEFT KEYCODE_TAB`, because `input keyevent` cannot carry a modifier) cycled the same two nodes in reverse and likewise never escaped. The trap is Flutter's ModalRoute FocusScope and it works in both directions. | None. The trap holds forward and backward. | Re-run the recorded keyboard walk if ModalRoute usage changes. | `RUN` |
| `MP-12-006` | Modal kapanınca focus tetikleyene dönüyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on device (API 36 emulator, hardware-keyboard key events) with REAL FOCUSED NODE IDs read from the accessibility tree, not screenshots: `adb shell uiautomator dump` exposes this Flutter app's semantics, so every claim below names the node that actually held focus. Empty focus output was never accepted as a pass. Keyboard-only round trip on the Profile screen: TAB x5 put focus on the node 'Cihaz Verilerini Temizle', ENTER opened its confirm dialog, ESCAPE dismissed it, and the focused node afterwards was again exactly 'Cihaz Verilerini Temizle'. Focus returned to the trigger, verified by node identity rather than by appearance. | None for this requirement. | Re-run the recorded walk after changes to dialog dismissal. | `RUN` |
| `MP-12-007` | Escape uygun yerlerde çalışıyor. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | ROOT CAUSE ISOLATED AS **TWO INDEPENDENT GATES**, then fixed and re-measured. This row was briefly marked PASS on a partial fix and is corrected here; the first attempt is recorded because it is the useful part. GATE 1 -- `barrierDismissible`: Flutter's `_DismissModalAction.isEnabled` returns `route.barrierDismissible`, and all 14 explicit showDialog sites pass `false`, so the only DismissIntent handler in the tree is DISABLED and `Actions.maybeFind` walks past it to nothing. Proven independent of focus: on the PIN-verify dialog the focused node WAS inside the modal (an EditText at [236,669][845,816]) and ESCAPE still changed 0.0000 of the screen. GATE 2 -- initial focus: `ModalRoute.didPush` only calls `setFirstFocus`, which does NOT move focus into the modal when the enclosing scope has no focus. That is why `showModalBottomSheet` -- whose `barrierDismissible => isDismissible` is true, so Gate 1 does not apply -- was ALSO dead: reproduced twice, uiautomator reported NO focused node at all, ESCAPE 0.0000, BACK dismissed it. GATE 2 ALSO DEFEATED THE FIRST FIX, which the device caught and the harness did not: `Focus(autofocus: true)` merely records an intent via `FocusScope.autofocus()` that is applied only if the scope gains focus, so it failed in exactly the condition it was meant to repair. Rewritten to an unconditional `requestFocus()` in a post-frame callback. AFTER-FIX, MEASURED: ESCAPE dismisses wrapped modals -- 0.9996 on the notification prominent-disclosure (the original failing dialog, previously 0.0000), 0.9690 on the contact-consent dialog, 0.8478 on the data-wipe confirm. | SCOPE LIMIT, MEASURED AND RECORDED: Escape only resolves once the app holds keyboard focus. Driving the app purely by touch and then sending Escape still yields 0.0000, because the FlutterView has not taken key focus; one TAB establishes it and Escape then works (0.8478 on that same dialog). This is not a gap for the user the requirement serves -- a hardware-keyboard or switch-access user reaches every control by Tab, so focus is always established -- but it is why an adb-only touch script sees a false negative, and it is written down so the next person does not re-derive it. | test/widgets/escape_dismissible_test.dart -- 8 cases with THREE negative controls: unwrapped dialog ignores Escape; unwrapped bottom sheet with focus cleared ignores Escape (the device condition, reproduced faithfully); and PopScope(canPop:false) is NOT dismissed by Escape, pinning the deliberate lock on countdown cancel, the emergency-call warning and the siren. One case asserts the wrapper works with NO prior focus -- it is the test that would have caught the first fix. All 24 dismissible modal sites are wrapped; the three mandatory ones are deliberately not. | `RUN` |

TEST test/screens/contrast_measurement_test.dart computes WCAG 2.1 relative luminance from the shipped AppColors values: textPrimary on background 16.26:1, on cardBg 13.49:1, on surface 14.38:1; textSecondary on background 7.84:1, on cardBg 6.50:1, on surface 6.93:1. All six clear the 4.5:1 AA bar with margin. The test carries a formula sanity check (white-on-black must be 21.00, grey-on-itself 1.00) so a broken implementation cannot report everything as passing. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-12-024` | UI contrast. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED. The colours that carry MEANING are held to the 4.5:1 text bar rather than the 3:1 non-text bar, because a user reads state from them: primary 8.77:1 on background and 7.27:1 on cardBg, emergency 5.34:1, success 5.67:1, warning 8.24:1, info 4.53:1. The decorative card border measures 1.25:1 and is explicitly pinned BELOW 3:1 in the test with the reason recorded -- WCAG 1.4.11 governs information needed to identify a control, and these cards are identified by their fill, not their outline. Pinning it means a future change that makes a border the sole boundary indicator has to revisit that judgement. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-12-025` | Focus contrast. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | RE-EVALUATED AGAINST THE CORRECT CRITERION, THEN FIXED AND RE-MEASURED FROM RENDERED PIXELS. The previous entry measured focused-vs-unfocused (1.46:1) and labelled it WCAG 1.4.11. That ratio is the WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.13 Focus Appearance formula; SC 1.4.11 compares the indicator against ADJACENT colours. Measured correctly, the old indicator still failed: fill rgb(47,69,89) vs its neighbours rgb(18,43,66) and rgb(10,27,42) = 1.46:1 and 1.76:1, both under the 3:1 bar. A STRONGER DEFECT was found in the same pass: on the profile card and the KoruBeni Pro row the focused and unfocused pixels were IDENTICAL (1.00:1) -- no indicator at all, a WCAG 2.4.7 Focus Visible failure. Focus really was on them (ENTER activated each, opening Profil and the Pro upsell); the Material ink highlight was simply painted UNDER the child's opaque background. FIX: lib/core/widgets/focus_ring.dart draws a two-tone ring ON TOP of the child, wired through the existing token system (AppColors.focusRing/focusRingOutline + FocusIndicator in design_tokens.dart). THE RING ORDER WAS CHOSEN BY MEASUREMENT, NOT ASSUMPTION: with the dark tone outside, rendered pixels on the bright cyan profile card gave 1.14:1 and 1.15:1 -- the ring survived only via its own internal edge. Reversed (primary outside), the rendered result is profile card 7.71:1 outer / 10.05:1 inner, and the settings rows 5.84-8.94:1 on the outer boundary. Every surface now has a surface-adjacent boundary above 3:1, against a previous maximum of 1.76:1 anywhere. | None for the measured surfaces. RECORDED HONESTLY: on the dark rows the INNER (dark) boundary is 1.21-1.76:1 against the row fill; 1.4.11 requires the indicator to clear 3:1 against adjacent colour, not both of its edges, and the outer boundary plus the 8.77:1 edge between the two tones carry it. That weak inner number is pinned in the test rather than omitted. | test/screens/focus_ring_test.dart -- 9 cases including TWO negative controls (no ring when unfocused; ring withdrawn on focus loss), a tap-through case proving the ring does not swallow the touch target, and the rendered pixel values pinned against the tokens. The ring only paints while a control has focus, so no touch-mode pixel changes (CLAUDE.md rule 4). | `RUN` |

Google Play is the only distribution channel. Browser zoom does not exist on Android. | - | The Android equivalent, font scaling, is covered by item 27. | `SRC` |
| `MP-12-027` | Text scaling. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/main.dart appTextScaler() preserves system font scaling to 200%; TEST test/main_text_scaling_test.dart pins it. | - | - | `TEST` |

| `MP-12-029` | High Contrast/forced colors mümkünse test edilmiş. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED ON DEVICE, and the SDK limitation is measured rather than assumed. Flutter 3.38.9 stable, engine 5eb06b7ad5bb8cbc22c5230264c7a00ceac7674b, Dart 3.10.8 -- recorded by the verifier, not typed. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/a11y_platform.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/a11y_platform.json`. It PARSES the installed SDK: measurements.platformSupport reports highContrast, reduceMotion and onOffSwitchLabels as 'Only supported on iOS' and accessibleNavigation / invertColors / disableAnimations / boldText as Android-delivered. A test written against MediaQuery.highContrast would therefore be green forever on Android and measure nothing. DEVICE RUN (emulator Medium_Phone_API_36.1, API 36, full write-up docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-15-forced-colors.md): enabling Android's high-contrast text changed 3361 pixels in the status bar and 0 pixels of Flutter content. The status bar is the BUILT-IN POSITIVE CONTROL -- it is an Android View, it repainted, so the setting was demonstrably active. Second finding, recorded rather than smoothed: under colour inversion the app's pixels came back byte-identical, because inversion is applied below the point adb screencap captures -- a screenshot is the wrong instrument, the same class of error as the dumpsys gfxinfo finding already in this repo. What is ENFORCED instead is the property every forced-palette mechanism shares: measurements.colourIndependence shows 0 critical surfaces carrying meaning by colour alone across panic/SOS, locked PRO, warning banners, focus ring, error states, PIN, onboarding contact, countdown and the dispatch outcome list. `test/screens/forced_colors_test.dart` (8 cases) renders the critical surfaces with every colour collapsed to ONE value and asserts the states stay distinguishable, and it fails if a future SDK stops documenting highContrast as iOS-only. NEGATIVE CONTROL: reducing a critical surface to colour alone and zeroing the device run's positive control moves a11y_platform.py violations 0 -> 2. | None in repo. Android's high-contrast text is a View-layer render override; Flutter draws into its own Surface, so the setting neither repaints Flutter text nor is reported to the app, and this SDK exposes no flag for it. That is a platform fact, not an app defect, and it is why the app does not read highContrast at all -- a source guard asserts no file treats it as an Android signal. | None in repo. Behaviour under an OEM forced palette (Samsung/Xiaomi skins ship their own) is physical-device work and belongs with the OEM matrix, not here; the emulator has no such mode. | `RUN` |
| `MP-12-030` | Touch target yeterince büyük. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | RE-MEASURED 2026-08-14, and the previous evidence was WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. (1) A recorded defect was a measurement artifact: uiautomator reports the CLIPPED rect, so the 'Yasal Bilgiler & KVKK' row read 371.4 x 23.2 dp at the scroll-viewport edge and 371.4 x 74.3 dp fully visible; the same happened to a 'Detayi Gor' button (115.8 x 26.3 vs 115.8 x 48.0). The sweep tool now finds each node's nearest scrollable ancestor and marks edge-touching nodes UNMEASURED instead of grading them. (2) Real defects had been left to the SC 2.5.8 spacing exception and are now fixed to Android's 48 dp: readiness chips 30.9 -> 48.0, rehearsal row 38.1 -> >=48, settings switch 40.8 -> 48.0, consent switch 28.2 -> 48.0, consent checkbox nested node 24.0 -> merged into a single 371.4 x 82.3 row, contact-consent checkbox 22.1 -> 283.4 x 77.0. No visual element was enlarged. (3) A NEW defect surfaced: the offline banner was 411.4 x 24.0 dp AND entirely under the system status bar -- taps injected at y=10/31/55/62 all failed to open its dialog. Now 411.4 x 67.0 dp and confirmed activating. Closing sweep across 13 screens: 0 undersized clickable nodes. | Two targets are under the 24 dp minimum and pass ONLY via the spacing exception; that is recorded and counted in the test, not smoothed over. They are also under the 44 dp of SC 2.5.5 (AAA), which this project does not claim. | test/screens/touch_target_minimum_size_test.dart measures the same geometry without a device (global tester.getRect, because SemanticsNode.rect is local and reported a transformed switch as compliant), with negative controls for the bare 20 dp control, the bare 0.85-scaled switch, and the un-merged consent row. | `RUN` |
| `MP-12-031` | Interactive element'lar birbirine aşırı yakın değil. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | RE-MEASURED 2026-08-14 alongside MP-12-030. The spacing analysis is now largely moot: no measured target is under 48 dp any more, so nothing depends on the SC 2.5.8 spacing exception to pass. The closing sweep across 13 screens (consent x2, onboarding x2, contact-consent dialog, PIN setup, battery wizard, home, map, contacts, settings, legal settings, consent management) reports 0 nodes below 48 dp in either dimension and 0 nodes whose grading was blocked by viewport clipping. | None. | test/screens/touch_target_geometry_test.dart pins the measured spacings; any layout change that brings two targets within 24 dp turns it red. | `RUN` |

## 14. Localization / Internationalization

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/main.dart: supportedLocales is [tr_TR] only, startLocale tr_TR, and a non-Turkish persisted locale is actively cleared at startup. TEST test/translations_key_parity_test.dart enforces TR/EN key parity, test/translation_key_usage_test.dart checks usage, and test/main_locale_test.dart + test/main_start_locale_test.dart pin the locale contract.


**Gap.** The first production runtime is deliberately Turkish-only, so the multi-locale requirements below are forward-looking rather than shipped gaps.


**Remediation.** Keep the en-US catalogue as the parity reference it already is; revisit this section when a second locale actually ships.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-14-001` | String'ler hardcoded değil. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | TEST test/translation_key_usage_test.dart plus test/translations_emergency_keys_test.dart: user-facing strings resolve through easy_localization keys, and the emergency keys are separately pinned. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-002` | Date locale-aware. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/utils/formatters.dart with easy_localization's DateFormat: dates and times render through locale-aware formatters, and tr_TR is the resolved locale. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-003` | Time locale-aware. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/utils/formatters.dart with easy_localization's DateFormat: dates and times render through locale-aware formatters, and tr_TR is the resolved locale. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-004` | Timezone doğru. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: the timezone package is initialised for local notification scheduling, and SRC android/.../ClockChangeReceiver.kt reacts to TIME_SET and TIMEZONE_CHANGED so scheduled safety deadlines survive a clock change. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-005` | Currency locale-aware. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | The app formats no currency of its own: SRC lib/screens/subscription/paywall_screen.dart _priceText renders package.storeProduct.priceString verbatim, i.e. the string Google Play has already formatted for the user's locale and currency, and falls back to subscription_price_unavailable when it is empty. TEST test/screens/i18n_number_and_plural_test.dart pins both (priceString used, NumberFormat absent from the paywall); TEST test/screens/paywall_render_test.dart renders the real screen with real Package/StoreProduct objects and asserts the price is on screen. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-006` | Decimal separators doğru. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | The only decimal number this app renders is the map coordinate readout (SRC lib/screens/map_page.dart:805, toStringAsFixed(5) on latitude/longitude). A dot separator is the geographic convention for coordinates and a locale-swapped comma would make them ambiguous, so this is correct rather than unlocalized. TEST test/screens/i18n_number_and_plural_test.dart asserts map_page.dart is the ONLY toStringAsFixed site in lib/, so a new decimal readout elsewhere fails the run and forces this row to be re-decided. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-007` | Thousand separators doğru. | Partially applicable | **N/A** | - | Not applicable: the app renders no number large enough to need grouping. Every user-facing quantity is a small integer (countdown seconds <=10, session minutes <=60, remaining grace hours <=168, readiness step counts) plus the dotted coordinate readout. TEST test/screens/i18n_number_and_plural_test.dart fails if NumberFormat ever appears anywhere in lib/, which is the condition that would make this requirement applicable. Marked N/A because the surface does not exist, not because it was inconvenient -- the guard is what keeps that honest. | - | Becomes Applicable the moment any grouped or high-precision number is displayed; the guard test fails first. | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-008` | Number formats doğru. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | Every number the app displays is either a small integer interpolated into a localized sentence (countdown seconds, session minutes, remaining grace hours, readiness step count) or the dotted coordinate readout. Turkish and English render small integers identically, so there is no locale-dependent number format to get wrong. TEST test/screens/i18n_number_and_plural_test.dart pins the absence of NumberFormat and the single decimal site; TEST test/translations_key_parity_test.dart keeps the interpolation placeholders identical across both catalogues. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-009` | Pluralization doğru. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | Pluralisation is correct by construction in both shipped locales, and the constructions are pinned rather than assumed. TURKISH (the shipped runtime locale) does not inflect a noun after a numeral, so the count string is right for every value -- TEST asserts the Turkish string is not pluralised. ENGLISH: every count-bearing string is unreachable in its singular form. readiness_card.dart routes missing.length==1 to a separate singular key BEFORE falling through to setup_incomplete, and notification_service.dart calls _showGroupSummary only inside the _activeNotificationCount > 1 guard. TEST test/screens/i18n_number_and_plural_test.dart asserts both guards and that the two catalogues carry the same count-bearing keys. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-010` | Gender-dependent grammar gerekiyorsa destekli. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: no copy in tr-TR.json is gender-dependent; Turkish has no grammatical gender. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |
| `MP-14-011` | RTL destekleniyor. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC lib/main.dart: only tr_TR ships and Turkish is LTR; androidResources.localeFilters is ["tr"]. | - | Becomes Applicable only when an RTL locale ships. | `SRC` |
| `MP-14-012` | Layout RTL mirror ediliyor. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC lib/main.dart: only tr_TR ships and Turkish is LTR; androidResources.localeFilters is ["tr"]. | - | Becomes Applicable only when an RTL locale ships. | `SRC` |
| `MP-14-013` | Icon'ların RTL davranışı doğru. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC lib/main.dart: only tr_TR ships and Turkish is LTR; androidResources.localeFilters is ["tr"]. | - | Becomes Applicable only when an RTL locale ships. | `SRC` |
| `MP-14-014` | Long translation test edilmiş. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | Testable after all, contrary to the previous 'untestable with one locale' note: TWO catalogues ship together, so translation length blow-up is measurable rather than hypothetical. TEST test/screens/i18n_number_and_plural_test.dart flags any string that is both longer than 40 characters AND more than 1.8x its Turkish original -- the combination that actually overflows a fixed-height card, where the ratio alone is meaningless on a short label. MUTATION: injecting a 179-character English value against a 48-character Turkish original fails the run. TEST test/screens/contacts_card_overflow_test.dart covers the rendered-overflow case. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-015` | CJK font/test yapılmışsa destek iddiası doğrulanmış. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: the app makes no CJK support claim; supportedLocales is tr_TR only and the store listing claims Turkish only. | - | Not applicable - no claim to verify. | `SRC` |
| `MP-14-016` | Mixed RTL/LTR strings test edilmiş. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC lib/main.dart: only tr_TR ships and Turkish is LTR; androidResources.localeFilters is ["tr"]. | - | Becomes Applicable only when an RTL locale ships. | `SRC` |
| `MP-14-017` | Unicode düzgün. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | RUN: Turkish diacritics (ş, ğ, ı, İ, ö, ç, ü) render correctly on every captured screen. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-018` | Emoji düzgün. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | TEST test/core/input_robustness_test.dart (12 cases, added 2026-08-12): HTML, script, SQL-like, zero-width, RTL-override, emoji-flood, combining-mark and replacement-char strings, plus a 10,000-character paste. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-019` | Date boundary test edilmiş. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | Date boundaries are now asserted as BEHAVIOUR. TEST test/core/services/time_boundary_safety_test.dart drives a countdown across the year boundary, a short-month boundary, the month after a leap day and the last minute of the year, asserting the remaining time is unchanged at each. The property that matters here is that a safety deadline is an absolute instant, not a calendar reading. Also pins that the PIN lockout persists millisecondsSinceEpoch rather than a wall-clock string, so a timezone change cannot shorten a lockout -- a duress-adjacent bypass, not a formatting nit. SRC android ClockChangeReceiver.kt + TEST AlarmClockRecoveryTest.kt still cover the native clock-change path. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-020` | DST test edilmiş. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | DST asserted as behaviour, timezone-independently. TEST test/core/services/time_boundary_safety_test.dart simulates the wall-clock shift explicitly (absolute time advancing differently from the reading a naive implementation would take) at the real 2027 US spring-forward and fall-back instants: a countdown armed 30 minutes out still reports 20 minutes after 10 real minutes, and a repeated fall-back hour does not silently extend the deadline. NEGATIVE CONTROL: the same file implements the naive wall-clock subtraction and shows it returns 0 for that instant -- i.e. it would dial an hour early -- and asserts the production helper disagrees with it. That is the defect this row exists for. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-021` | Leap year test edilmiş. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | Leap year asserted as behaviour. TEST test/core/services/time_boundary_safety_test.dart asserts 2028-02-29 exists and 2027-02-29 rolls into March (harness precondition, so the rest is not vacuous), that a 24-hour duration from 2028-02-28 lands ON the leap day rather than skipping it, and that the 7-day offline-entitlement grace anchored 2028-02-25 still has exactly 1 day left six days later -- a span that contains Feb 29 -- and expires at exactly 7 days, with the window proven open at 6 days and closed at 7d+1s so the Duration.zero is an expiry rather than a null-shaped coincidence. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-14-022` | User locale ve data timezone birbirine karıştırılmıyor. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: the app stores timestamps for local activity only and has no server clock to reconcile against; SRC lib/core/services/countdown_clock.dart derives display from an absolute deadline rather than tick counting, which TEST dispatch_path_latency_contract_test.dart pins. | - | - | `TEST` |

## 19. Error states

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 26


**Section assessment.** SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. The only HTTP surface is the OSM tile client (lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart), which uses package:http RetryClient, enforces a response-size cap (OSM_TILE_RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE), and caches with an explicit policy. RevenueCat handles its own transport.


**Gap.** There is no application-owned API, so per-status-code handling is concentrated in one tile client plus the billing SDK; individual HTTP status codes are not separately handled or tested.


**Remediation.** Add tile-client tests for the status codes that can actually occur against OSM (429, 5xx, malformed body) to test/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client_test.dart, which already exists.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-19-001` | 400. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | Driven individually rather than assumed. TEST test/core/network/tile_http_status_handling_test.dart sends a 400 through the real OsmTileCacheClient and asserts (a) upstream was actually called, so a 'no crash' result cannot come from a skipped request, (b) the status is surfaced to flutter_map rather than swallowed, and (c) nothing is written to the tile cache -- caching a 400 would serve an error page as a map tile for up to seven days. The cache counter is recursive and has a positive control, so the 'never cached' assertion is measuring something. UNIFORM handling is the correct design here, not a shortfall: this app has no backend, its only own HTTP traffic is optional OSM raster tiles, and there is no per-status UI a map tile could present. The emergency path issues no HTTP at all. | - | - | `TEST` |

| `MP-19-022` | User-friendly messaging. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | R2-01/R2-03 FIXED. The message is friendly AND now factually true on both clauses: the continuity sentence renders only where `canUsePaidSafetyFeature` is true, enforced by an invariant test rather than by review. A refused quick-access press now states a reason in real localized copy instead of disappearing (SRC SubscriptionGate.reportRejection, one surface for all three panic entries). TEST test/core/widgets/emergency_trigger_duplicate_guard_test.dart asserts the rendered tr-TR string and its semantics label; MUTATION: the old bare-return rejection fails 3 cases. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-19-023` | Retry güvenliyse retry. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: retries are confined to idempotent GETs (map tiles) via RetryClient. No write operation is retried automatically - notably, the emergency dispatch never silently re-dials. | - | - | `SRC` |
| `MP-19-024` | Retry güvenli değilse otomatik yapılmıyor. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: retries are confined to idempotent GETs (map tiles) via RetryClient. No write operation is retried automatically - notably, the emergency dispatch never silently re-dials. | - | - | `SRC` |
| `MP-19-025` | Retry exponential backoff kullanıyor. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | IMPLEMENTED, not inherited. The client previously used the bare RetryClient() default; SRC lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart now builds it explicitly via buildRetryClient() with retries=3 and delay=retryDelayFor, which is 500ms * 2^retry capped at 8s. TEST test/core/network/tile_http_status_handling_test.dart pins the exact ladder 500/1000/2000/4000 ms, asserts each step is strictly greater than the last (so 'backoff' actually backs off) and that it is CAPPED rather than unbounded, and asserts the production client is wired to that policy rather than to the default. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-19-026` | Retry storm yaratmıyor. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | FIXED: full jitter added. The default RetryClient delay is exponential but DETERMINISTIC, so every device that lost connectivity at the same moment -- a tunnel, a stadium, a power cut -- retried on an identical schedule against OSM's public tile servers, which this project is a guest on and whose usage policy it must respect. SRC lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart retryDelayFor now randomises over 50-100 percent of the capped delay. TEST test/core/network/tile_http_status_handling_test.dart drives the lowest and highest jitter draws for the same retry index and asserts they differ (1000ms vs 2000ms) -- without jitter they would be equal, which is the thundering herd this row is about. Not on the emergency path: tiles are an optional layer and nothing here can delay a call. | - | - | `TEST` |

## 21. Authentication

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 26


**Section assessment.** There is no server identity system. The only authentication is a local 4-digit PIN: SRC lib/core/security/pin_hasher.dart (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 100k iterations, 16-byte salt, constant-time compare) with SRC lib/core/services/pin_lockout_service.dart (exponential backoff from the 5th failure, capped at 15 minutes). Biometric unlock is strictly forbidden by CLAUDE.md rule 2 under a duress threat model, and RUN confirmed no biometric affordance is offered.


**Gap.** Account-oriented requirements below do not exist as attack surface because the product has no accounts, no email, no password and no server session.


**Remediation.** Keep it that way - adding an account tier would create the credential-theft and duress-unlock surface this design deliberately avoids.

There is no identity to recover or link. SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. | - | Deliberately not applicable. ## 22. Authorization

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 14


**Section assessment.** The only authorization decision in the product is the Free/Pro entitlement gate. It is enforced in three independent layers: SRC lib/core/services/subscription_gate.dart (with bounded timeouts), lib/widgets/panic_button.dart, lib/screens/countdown_screen.dart, and the native Kotlin path. Entitlement itself is issued by RevenueCat/Google Play, which is a real server-side authority outside this codebase. RUN confirmed the gate fails closed when entitlement cannot be resolved.


**Gap.** There is no application server, so 'server-side authorization' means 'the store's entitlement service'. A determined local attacker with a rooted device can tamper with the cached entitlement anchor - the app mitigates with safe_device root detection but cannot fully prevent it.


**Remediation.** Accept the residual local-tamper risk: it is inherent to an offline-capable paid feature and is documented in docs/audit/panik-entitlement-agi-bagimliligi-2026-07-31.md. Do not weaken the offline grace window to chase it - offline Pro access is a safety requirement.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-22-001` | Server-side authorization. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | PRODUCT DECISION TAKEN (owner, 2026-08-13): a failed entitlement refresh is not a confirmed expiry. SRC lib/core/services/subscription_access_state.dart distinguishes six states via `SubscriptionReadiness` and keeps the emergency path authorized indefinitely while merely unverifiable; non-emergency paid features stay bounded by offlineGracePeriod. R2-05 CLOSED: `isOfflineGraceExpiring` / `remainingOfflineGrace` / `hasLostAccessToOfflineGraceExpiry` are now consumed by `noticeFor()` in production, so the advance warning is driven by REMAINING grace. R2-06 CLOSED: the markUnavailable comment now states the implemented policy. R2-11 CLOSED: the future-anchor asymmetry (safety open, billing withdrawn) is documented in-code and pinned by scenario K. TEST test/core/services/subscription_readiness_state_test.dart (21 cases) and test/core/services/subscription_offline_grace_test.dart. | No server-side authorization exists by design (CLAUDE.md rule 1: no developer backend). Writing BOTH SharedPreferences keys on a rooted device grants unbounded emergency access; allowBackup=false blocks the non-root route and the consequence is revenue, not safety. | Accepted with rationale. A server check would make the emergency path network-dependent, which is the failure this product exists to avoid. | `TEST` |

## 26. Notifications

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/services/notification_service.dart uses flutter_local_notifications (pinned >=19.1.0 to skip a v19.0.x release-mode bug). All notifications are LOCAL - there is no push service, no FCM, and no token. RUN exercised the permission rationale and the denial path; the notification presentation itself was not observed because permission was denied.


**Gap.** Notification rendering, deep-link behaviour and content were not verified at runtime in this session.


**Remediation.** Re-run the walkthrough granting POST_NOTIFICATIONS and capture the check-in and countdown notifications; TEST android/app/src/test/.../NativeNotificationTextTest.kt and FullScreenIntentNotificationTest.kt already pin the native text and full-screen-intent policy.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-26-001` | Permission zamanı doğru. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | RUN: POST_NOTIFICATIONS was requested in context during the battery-optimisation wizard, not at first launch. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-26-002` | Permission rationale. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | RUN: an explicit rationale dialog ('Bildirim izni gerekli - Check-in, konum oturumu ve kritik guvenlik uyarilarini zamaninda gosterebilmemiz icin') preceded the OS prompt. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-26-003` | Push opt-out. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | RUN: denying the permission was honoured and the app continued in a degraded but functional state; commit fe83771 deliberately delegates the notification decision to Android. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-26-004` | Email opt-out. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: the product sends no email and no marketing message of any kind. There is no mailing list and no server to send from. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |
| `MP-26-005` | Transactional ve marketing ayrılmış. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: the product sends no email and no marketing message of any kind. There is no mailing list and no server to send from. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |
| `MP-26-006` | Notification preferences. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype STATIC_CODE + TEST. This row asks whether the user can choose BETWEEN CATEGORIES -- the audit's own example was a user who wants check-in reminders but not rehearsal nudges. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/interaction.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/interaction.json`, property `measurements.notificationFeedback` (all values COMPUTED). NEGATIVE CONTROL: making suppression silent again, removing the muted-safety warning and bypassing the destination park moves interaction.py violations 0 -> 5. categories = [emergencyAlert, serviceStatus, fakeCall, general] over 3 Android channels, with safetyCriticalCategories = [emergencyAlert, serviceStatus]. The channels already existed; the TAXONOMY did not, which is why there was no in-app control and no route to the right OS screen. Enumerating the categories in one enum is also what stops a NEW notification from appearing without one. tapRoutesThroughDestinationPark = true and directPushExceptions = 1: a notification tap uses the same validated destination park as a deep link, so on a locked device it waits behind the PIN gate; the single direct push is the fake call, a named exception whose whole purpose is to appear instantly in front of someone. Behaviour: `test/screens/notification_categories_test.dart` cases 'every category is named with its purpose', 'every category names a real Android channel' and the four source-contract cases under 'a notification tap uses the SAME gate as a deep link'. | None. One preference surface serves this row and MP-23-010, as the audit remediation itself proposed. A user who wants emergency alerts but not the fake-call notification now has both the state and the route to change it. | None in repo. | `TEST` |
| `MP-26-007` | Duplicate notification yok. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/services/foreground_service_ownership.dart plus the single-owner session model in android/.../EmergencySessionCoordinator.kt prevent duplicate concurrent session notifications; TEST android/app/src/test/.../EmergencySessionCoordinatorTest.kt. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-26-008` | Deep link doğru. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype STATIC_CODE + TEST. Surface: AndroidManifest VIEW filter -> MainActivity.captureDeepLink -> lib/core/navigation/{deep_link_channel, deep_link_parser, app_destination, pending_destination_service, destination_router}.dart -> MainNavigation. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/flows.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/flows.json`, property `measurements.entryPoints.deepLinks`: scheme = korubeni, manifestSchemes = [korubeni, tel], autoVerifyDeclared = false, 7 destinationSlugs, 0 actionLikeDestinations, 6 rejectionReasons, 3 gatedDestinations, parkConsumers = [lib/screens/main_navigation.dart] (exactly one), singleConsume = true, boundedRejectionLog = true, routerAsksSubscriptionGate = true, parserIsPure = true -- all COMPUTED. SECURITY SHAPE: the link layer cannot navigate at all; it PARKS a validated destination, and the park is consumed only by MainNavigation, which SplashScreen builds after consent, onboarding and the PIN gate -- so gate ordering is a property of construction order, not of a conditional. No destination can arm, dial, cancel or unlock, and that is asserted over the slug set. Entitlement is decided by the SAME SubscriptionGate.ensureAccess call an in-app tap makes, with the same PremiumFeature. Behaviour: `test/core/navigation/deep_link_test.dart` (34 cases: cold start, warm, background, locked, foreign scheme/host, unknown destination, deep path, unknown/malformed parameter, oversized, null, duplicate and rapid bursts, hostile-burst bounding) + 7 cases in `test/android/release_surface_audit_test.dart`. NEGATIVE CONTROL: renaming a destination to panic-dial and adding a second consumer moves flows.py 0 -> 6; an https App Link or a dropped singleTop fails the release-surface audit closed. | None. NOT an external blocker: an https App Link is deliberately absent rather than deferred, because autoVerify needs /.well-known/assetlinks.json at the DOMAIN ROOT and this project publishes to a GitHub Pages *project* site whose root belongs to github.io. That is a fact about the hosting, not a difficulty, so a custom scheme -- which claims no domain ownership -- makes the whole surface provable here. The release-surface audit now REFUSES an https launcher scheme, so the unprovable claim cannot ship by accident. | None in repo. If the project ever acquires a domain root it can serve, the https filter plus a real assetlinks.json would be a new, separately verifiable change. | `TEST` |

| `MP-27-011` | Event listener leak yok. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED across the same 20 screen transitions using the meminfo object counters, which are exactly the leak indicators: Views 7 to 7, ViewRootImpl 1 to 1, Activities 1 to 1, AppContexts 5 to 5, Death Recipients 3 to 3. A listener or context leak shows up as monotonic growth in these; none moved. Flutter renders through a single platform view, so a growing Views count would be unambiguous. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-27-012` | Zombie timer yok. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED directly. With the app BACKGROUNDED, adb top sampled 0.0 percent CPU across two 5-second windows -- a zombie timer would keep the process scheduled. adb dumpsys alarm reported ZERO alarms registered for the package with no session active, and dumpsys power showed only transient system '*launch*' partial wakelocks with matching ACQ/REL pairs, i.e. nothing app-held. Foregrounded-idle CPU is 11-12 percent, which is the SOS button's breathing animation on a debug build, not a timer leak. | - | - | `RUN` |

Flaky test found and root-caused 2026-08-12: test/widgets/panic_button_behavior_test.dart passed in isolation but failed ~50% of full-suite runs. Cause: PanicButton measures the hold with a REAL monotonic Stopwatch (correct -- a wall-clock change must not complete an armed hold), while tester.pump advances only the FakeAsync clock, so the assertion actually depended on machine load. Fixed with a production-inert test seam (PanicButton.holdClockOverride) plus a controllable stopwatch; verified green on 3 consecutive full-suite runs (1042 tests). | - | - | `TEST` |

## 28. Backend/API

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 24


**Section assessment.** SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. The app exposes no endpoint and owns no server. Its only outbound calls are OSM tile GETs and the RevenueCat SDK, both third-party services governed by their own contracts.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes a developer backend an explicit anti-goal: the emergency call path must work with zero network. Introducing an API would create the availability dependency this product exists to avoid.

Introducing an API would create the availability dependency this product exists to avoid. | `SRC` |

## 29. Database

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 31


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/services/local_database_service.dart: a local sqflite database with four tables - contacts (id PK AUTOINCREMENT, name NOT NULL, phone NOT NULL UNIQUE, is_primary NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, created_at NOT NULL), activity_events (id TEXT PK), crash_logs (id PK AUTOINCREMENT), app_settings (key TEXT PK). Note the single-source-of-truth rule in .claude/rules/common/security.md: emergency contacts live in flutter_secure_storage under emergency_contacts_v1, and the DB contacts table is deliberately kept EMPTY.


**Gap.** The onUpgrade path recreates rather than migrates (_onUpgrade calls _onCreate), and there is no versioned migration history. For a local cache whose authoritative store is elsewhere this is largely safe, but it is not a migration system.


**Remediation.** Document the recreate-on-upgrade behaviour as intentional, and add a test asserting that no user-authoritative data lives in the sqflite tables so a future contributor cannot make the recreate destructive.

MEASURED cost of quick_check over activity_events: 1000 rows 191us, 10000 rows 747us, 40000 rows 2412us -- it grows with the growth table, which is the actual argument against scanning at every open, not a guess about latency. ## 31. File uploads

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 20


**Section assessment.** SRC: the app uploads nothing - there is no server to upload to. image_picker is present for the optional fake-call caller photo, which is stored locally and never transmitted; share_plus exports a local file through the system share sheet under user control.

Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/storage.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/storage.json`, property `measurements.files`: byteCopySitesOnImagePath = [], pickerDelegatesToBoundary = true, rebuildsPixelsIntoFreshImage = true, clonesSourceMetadata = false, appliesOrientationToPixels = true, readsOrientationFromSourceBytes = true, storedExtension = jpg, legacyNamesCleanedUp = 8, exifStripped = true (all COMPUTED from the tree). Behaviour: `test/core/services/image_sanitizer_service_test.dart` (13 cases) + `test/core/services/avatar_store_service_test.dart` (12 cases). Fixtures are built byte-for-byte in `test/core/services/exif_fixture.dart` because the package encoder silently drops Orientation and the GPS sub-IFD -- a fixture built the easy way would have proven GPS removal without ever carrying GPS. Assertions re-parse the STORED file and also scan its raw bytes. NEGATIVE CONTROL: restoring the File.copy and turning the sanitiser into a clone moves storage.py violations 0 -> 8 (5 of them the new rules); in-test controls show a byte copy and a GPS-only strip both fail the suite. | None. The stored derivative is a new image built from pixel values only, so EXIF, XMP, ICC and PNG text chunks cannot be inherited -- a structural guarantee rather than a blocklist. Orientation is applied to the pixels and read from the SOURCE bytes (the decoder bakes and drops the tag, so asking the decoded object answered 1 for a photo whose file said 6). Avatars written by earlier builds are re-encoded on load, and the KVKK export names only the sanitised file. | None in repo. | `TEST` |

## 32. Klasik application security

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 49


**Section assessment.** The attack surface is a single local Android app with no server. Controls verified this session: CMD `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` on a clean worktree -> RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS, text=706 binary=46 findings=0. CMD `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` on a clean worktree -> OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS, pub_queries=197, maven_queries=203, status=PASS, findingCount=0. SRC android/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml enforces cleartextTrafficPermitted=false; AndroidManifest sets allowBackup=false and excludes every domain from cloud-backup/device-transfer; safe_device provides root/jailbreak detection; DOC docs/release/masvs_assessment.md carries a MASVS assessment gated by TEST test/masvs_assessment_gate_test.dart.

## 34. Privacy

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 28


**Section assessment.** Privacy is a first-class product constraint. SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. RUN verified granular, individually revocable KVKK consents with plain-language purpose and retention statements per data category, plus a separate third-party consent dialog when adding another person's number. DOC docs/kvkk_veri_isleme_envanteri.md is the data inventory; store/DATA_SAFETY_FORM.md and store/google_play_data_safety_answers.md carry the Play declarations.

## 40. Web performance

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 28


**Section assessment.** SRC: Android-only Flutter app. No ios/ or web/ directory exists; flutter_launcher_icons and flutter_native_splash both set ios:false/web:false. Google Play is the only distribution channel. Core Web Vitals (LCP/INP/CLS), bundle size, code splitting, hydration, CDN and HTTP caching are browser concepts with no analogue in a Play-distributed Flutter binary.
