## 41. Native/mobile performance

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** The performance budget that matters is defined in .claude/rules/common/performance.md as 'panic press -> dialling, with screen off, Doze active, and OEM battery savers hostile'. TEST test/screens/dispatch_path_latency_contract_test.dart pins the structural side of that budget (no fixed delay before arming, no transition animation on the dispatch path, haptics not awaited). A nightly emulator matrix (.github/workflows/nightly-android.yml, API 29-36) exercises connected safety tests.


**Gap.** No timing, memory, CPU, GPU or battery measurement from a physical device is recorded for the current build. The project's own rules state that the Doze race is device-only and must never be faked in a unit test.


**Remediation.** Execute the existing store/REAL_DEVICE_QA_MATRIX.md on physical hardware and record results under docs/audit/, as the repository's own convention requires. Emulator results cannot substitute for the Doze and OEM-kill scenarios.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-41-001` | Cold startup. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on emulator Medium_Phone_API_36.1 (arm64 API 36) with adb am start -W, 3 runs, force-stop before each so every run is genuinely cold: TotalTime 2143 / 1352 / 1272 ms. The 2143 is the first launch after boot with a cold page cache; the steady-state cold start is ~1.3s, inside Android's 2-second feedback threshold and far inside the 5s Vitals 'slow cold start' bar. Measured on a DEBUG build, which is a pessimistic upper bound -- a release build is AOT-compiled and starts faster -- so a PASS here is conservative. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-002` | Warm startup. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED with adb am start -W after backgrounding and 'am kill', 3 runs: TotalTime 1268 / 1161 / 1194 ms. PRECONDITION VALIDATED: 'adb shell pidof' was checked before each run and reported no pid, so these are genuine warm starts and not the invalid TotalTime=0 that am kill returns when the process is still foregrounded (that mistake was made and caught during this measurement). Inside the 2-second threshold on a debug build. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-003` | Resume. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED with adb am start -W after KEYCODE_HOME, process alive, 3 runs: TotalTime 71 / 69 / 55 ms. Resume is two orders of magnitude below the cold path and far inside Android's 1.5s hot-start bar. Debug build, so again a pessimistic upper bound. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-004` | Memory. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: process memory, profile build. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-perf-resources.md section 2: PSS 169.8 -> 175.1 MB over 28 scrolls, Views 7->7, Activities 1->1, AppContexts 5->5. NEGATIVE CONTROL: the same script reports Views/Activities/AppContexts; a retained Activity would move them. | Emulator PSS moved 169 803 -> 175 138 kB across 28 scroll gestures while Views stayed at 7, Activities at 1 and AppContexts at 5 -- no retained-context leak. What the emulator cannot give is the ABSOLUTE resting footprint on real silicon. | Physical-device measurement; the emulator verdict (no leak signature) is retained rather than discarded. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-005` | Battery. | Applicable | **BLOCKED** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: emulator battery model. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-perf-resources.md section 5: batterystats reports a virtual 3000 mAh cell with zero drain. NEGATIVE CONTROL: Computed drain: 0, actual drain: 0 -- a virtual battery. | The irreducible remainder needs a system, device or account outside this repository. What COULD be measured here was, and is cited above. | See EXTERNAL_LAUNCH_BLOCKERS.md; nothing further is closable in-repo. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-006` | CPU. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: profile build on API 36 emulator. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-perf-resources.md section 3: 0.0% top / 0.2% cpuinfo at rest after interaction. NEGATIVE CONTROL: top/cpuinfo report the process by name; a busy loop would move them. | Emulator CPU read 0.0% (top) / 0.2% (cpuinfo) at rest after interaction. That answers 'is something spinning' -- correctly now that the seven ambient loops ignoring reduce-motion were fixed -- but gives no real-silicon profile under load. | Physical-device measurement. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-007` | GPU. | Applicable | **BLOCKED** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: emulator GPU attribution. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-perf-resources.md section 0: the emulator uses the host GPU, so app GPU memory reads as 0. NEGATIVE CONTROL: Graphics: 0 kB is the absence of a measurement, not a measurement of zero. | The irreducible remainder needs a system, device or account outside this repository. What COULD be measured here was, and is cited above. | See EXTERNAL_LAUNCH_BLOCKERS.md; nothing further is closable in-repo. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-008` | Network. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: dumpsys netstats for the app uid. Verifier `scripts/measure_device_resources.sh` -> `docs/audit/evidence/storage.json`, property `measurements.cache.stampedeRisk` = none: there is no shared origin to stampede. The only network cache is per-tile against a public tile server, fetched by one process on one device, and the emergency path performs zero network I/O.. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-perf-resources.md section 4: no app-uid traffic recorded along the emergency path. NEGATIVE CONTROL: netstats shows system-uid traffic in the same window, so the instrument is not blind. | None. The cited property is the measurement, and the negative control shows the verifier can report the opposite. | Re-run `scripts/measure_device_resources.sh` to reproduce; the artifact carries the code revision it was measured against. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-009` | Frame drops. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: 744 frame intervals, profile build, 60Hz. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-a11y-perf.md section 'Kare sureleri': budget 16.67ms; a genuinely dropped frame at 60Hz appears as a ~33ms interval and none occurred in any run. NEGATIVE CONTROL: gfxinfo reported 7 frames for the same run and was rejected as the wrong instrument. | 744 frame intervals on the emulator produced no >=2x-budget interval; a dropped frame at 60Hz shows as ~33ms and none occurred. The 17-20ms spread is emulator present-time jitter. | Physical-device frame capture. This session could not re-capture SurfaceFlinger latency (the BLAST layer id churns per process start); the cited numbers are from the 2026-08-14 pass and attributed to that tree. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-010` | Scroll jank. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: scroll interaction, profile build. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-a11y-perf.md section 'Kare sureleri': across 744 intervals no interaction produced a >=2x-budget interval; the 17-20ms spread is emulator present-time jitter, and the reproducible p90 rise to 18.3-18.7ms during transitions was examined and is not a dropped frame. NEGATIVE CONTROL: gfxinfo reported 7 frames for the same run and was rejected as the wrong instrument for a Flutter BLAST layer. | Same 744-interval capture, scroll specifically: the reproducible p90 rise from 16.7 to 18.3-18.7ms during transitions was examined and is not a dropped frame. Scroll jank on a real panel with a real GPU is a different measurement. | Physical-device measurement. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-011` | Image memory. | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype PERFORMANCE. Surface: image/graphics memory, profile build. docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-perf-resources.md section 2: Native Heap 28.8 -> 30.2 MB across 28 scrolls while Dalvik fell 5.4 -> 2.7 MB (a GC in the same window); the app ships four asset files and decodes no user images, so there is no image cache to grow. NEGATIVE CONTROL: the same script reports Views/Activities/AppContexts; a retained Activity would move them. | Native heap moved 28 824 -> 30 204 kB across the scroll run while Dalvik fell 5 432 -> 2 664 kB in the same window (a GC), so no unbounded image-cache growth. But `Graphics: 0 kB` means graphics memory is NOT attributed to the app at all. | Physical-device measurement, where Graphics/EGL mtrack is real. | `RUN` |
| `MP-41-012` | Background activity. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/services/foreground_service.dart (specialUse foreground service), battery_optimization_service.dart, doze-aware exact alarms via android/.../AlarmDeadlineClock.kt, and BootCompletedReceiver restoring sessions after reboot. TEST android/app/src/test/.../DirectBootSafetyKernelTest.kt and AlarmClockRecoveryTest.kt. | - | - | `TEST` |
| `MP-41-013` | App switcher. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC lib/core/widgets/app_privacy_shield.dart: the app applies a privacy shield so safety content is not exposed in the app switcher. | - | - | `TEST` |

TEST android/app/src/test/.../DeviceProtectedEmergencySessionStoreTest.kt. | - | - | `TEST` |

## 42. Scalability

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 26


**Section assessment.** SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. There is no shared service to load, saturate or autoscale. Each installation is an independent single-user device; adding a millionth user adds no load to any component the project operates.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The only shared dependencies are OpenStreetMap tiles and RevenueCat, both third-party services with their own capacity. The tile client's persistent cache and size cap are the relevant courtesy controls and they exist (section 30).

| `MP-42-024` | Dependency quotas. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype STATIC_CODE + TEST. Surface: lib/core/network/osm_tile_cache_client.dart, instrumented at the boundary that actually talks to the tile server. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/storage.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/storage.json`, property `measurements.cache.tileRequestVolume`: 3 counters (tileRequests / tileRequestsServedLocally / upstreamTileRequests), dedupesInFlight = true, cacheServesRepeatViews = true, sessionModel viewports 4 / tileSidePx 256 / keepBuffer 1, policyCeilingAsserted = true -- all COMPUTED. Three counters rather than one because 'how many tiles did a session need' and 'how many times did we hit OSM' are different numbers and only the second is the quota figure. MEASURED SESSION (`test/core/network/tile_request_volume_test.dart`, 5 cases): the viewport tile count is DERIVED from the shipped map config (minZoom 3, maxZoom 18, read from map_page.dart so a map change invalidates the figure) and a real device's metrics -- 1080x2400 at DPR 2.75 gives 35 tiles per viewport. A representative session (open the map, zoom twice to street level, pan one screen = 4 viewports) requested 140 tiles and cost 140 upstream requests worst case. Re-opening the same area costs 0. Eight simultaneous asks for one tile -- what a fast pan produces -- cost 1. The assertion is an order-of-magnitude bound against OSM's named heavy-use ceiling (>250 tiles/sec), not a golden count: pinning the exact number would break on any map-layout change while saying nothing about the quota. NEGATIVE CONTROL: removing the counters moves storage.py violations 0 -> 13. | None. The figure exists, is derived from the shipped configuration rather than chosen, and is comparable to OSM's stated policy. | None in repo. | `TEST` |

## 43. Reliability

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 31


**Section assessment.** The reliability question for this product is inverted: because the emergency path has no developer backend (CLAUDE.md rule 1), no service outage can prevent a panic call. The failure domains that DO exist are device-local - process death, reboot, Doze, revoked permissions - and each has an explicit native control plus tests: android/.../DeviceProtectedEmergencySessionStore.kt, BootCompletedReceiver, CountdownAlarmReceiver ('C4 safety net: fires if Dart timer freezes under Doze'), ExactAlarmPermissionReceiver, ClockChangeReceiver.

## 44. Observability

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 39


**Section assessment.** Observability is deliberately local-only. SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. SRC lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart records allowlisted structured LocalErrorCode values on-device; lib/core/services/crash_log_service.dart writes to the local crash_logs table; android/.../NativeSafetyEventRing.kt keeps a native safety event ring (TEST NativeSafetyEventRingTest.kt); lib/core/services/health_check_service.dart and startup_diagnostics_service.dart provide local self-checks.


**Gap.** There is no remote telemetry of any kind, so the operator cannot observe production error rates, crash rates, latency or adoption. Every requirement in this section that presumes a central collector is unmet by design.


**Remediation.** This is a deliberate KVKK trade-off, not an oversight - but it must be accepted explicitly, because it means the only production signal available is Play Console vitals (crash/ANR rates, which Google provides without an SDK) plus user reports. Document that dependency, and make Play Console vitals monitoring an explicit named operational duty in the launch runbook.

## 45. SLO / alerting

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** No SLI, SLO, error budget, alert, synthetic monitor, uptime check or status page exists for this product.


**Gap.** There is no service to measure availability of, so classic SLOs do not transfer - but the product does have one measurable objective it currently does not track: the panic-press-to-dial budget defined in .claude/rules/common/performance.md, and the Play Console crash/ANR rates that Google publishes without an SDK.


**Remediation.** Define two concrete objectives that are actually observable for this architecture: (1) Play Console crash-free session rate and ANR rate thresholds, with a named owner who checks them on a stated cadence after each release; (2) the panic-press-to-dial latency budget, verified per release on physical hardware rather than continuously monitored. Write both into docs/release/ alongside the existing safety case.

## 47. Manual QA

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 29


**Section assessment.** DOC store/QA_SENARYOLAR.md, store/MANUAL_SMOKE_TEST_SCRIPT.md and store/REAL_DEVICE_QA_MATRIX.md define the manual QA programme, and docs/qa/ holds dated evidence from earlier phases (phase3-emulator, phase3-physical-preflight, phase5-*). RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). covered the fresh-user path this session.


**Gap.** The recorded manual-QA evidence predates the current HEAD, and several personas in this checklist (paid user, expired subscriber) cannot be exercised without a Play test account.


**Remediation.** Re-run store/MANUAL_SMOKE_TEST_SCRIPT.md against the release candidate and date the evidence into docs/qa/. Cover the paid/expired personas via the Play internal-test track.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-47-001` | Fresh user. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). This session exercised a genuinely fresh install from first launch. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-47-002` | Existing user. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on device: a returning user with existing data relaunching the app lands on the PIN unlock screen ('Uygulama kilidi. PIN ile acin.'), enters the PIN and reaches Home with the saved emergency contact present ('Acil kisi hazir / Ayse Yilmaz'). Also exercised after genuine process death (`am kill`, new pid): the reconstructed process ALSO landed on the PIN lock screen rather than restoring straight into the Contacts screen it died on -- the security-correct outcome for a duress model, since reconstructed state must not step around the lock. | None. | Covered by the recorded lifecycle matrix in docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-14-a11y-perf.md. | `RUN` |
| `MP-47-003` | Power user. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED, not asserted. Archetype UNIT_BEHAVIOR + PROCESS_ARTIFACT. Verifier `scripts/audit_evidence/storage.py` -> `docs/audit/evidence/storage.json`, property `measurements.schema.volumePaths`: powerUserSteps lists all six steps the audit named (arm, cancel, rehearse, export, revoke consent, re-consent), powerUserAutomated = test/core/services/power_user_path_test.dart, powerUserInManualScript = true, manualScriptNamesTheTest = true -- COMPUTED. The remediation asked to extend the manual script and run the path once. The manual row IS added to store/MANUAL_SMOKE_TEST_SCRIPT.md, but a one-off human run answers the question once and then rots, so the same path is also driven deterministically on every release. The defect the path guards against is a LATER step breaking an EARLIER one's record, so the steps run in sequence against shared state and each step's observable effect is asserted after the ones that follow: the timeline still holds BOTH the arm and the cancel, the rehearsal timestamp is unchanged, the export carries the consent log, and after re-consent a FRESH ConsentManager reading storage alone agrees -- the case that catches 'the cache was right, the durable log was not'. The append-only log's real failure mode is order, so revoke -> re-consent -> revoke -> re-consent -> revoke gets its own run and must end REVOKED. The database is integrity-checked after the whole journey. 5 cases. | None in repo. The manual row stays NOT_RUN in the script because a human pass notices things a test does not; that pass is part of the physical-device QA matrix, which is the standing external blocker, and it is no longer the ONLY thing answering this row. | Run the manual row once during the physical-device QA pass; the automated path already runs every release. | `TEST` |

The tiers are product-bound, not benchmark numbers: activity_events is the app's only unbounded table and a safety event is a human act, so 100 is a few months of ordinary use, 1000 is daily check-ins for about three years, and 10000 (~9 events a day for three years) is beyond any realistic user -- the worst REALISTIC supported volume. MEASURED at those tiers (`test/core/services/high_volume_timeline_test.dart`, 8 cases): full-table ordered query 3522 / 2461 / 7914 us for 100 / 1000 / 10000 rows -- growth no worse than proportional, asserted as a ratio rather than an absolute millisecond bound, which would be a CI flake; LIMIT 200 of 10000 = 3728 us; full integrity_check + foreign_key_check of 10000 rows = 2703 us; every tier returns every row, newest first, with no duplicates and no losses; a targeted delete touches exactly one of ten thousand. LAZINESS: a 10000-row list builds 12 widgets, scrolling to 25/50/99/100 percent raises no exception, the last row is reachable and correct, and the MP-10-023 identity anchor still resolves at the maximum tier. ## 48. CI pipeline

**Section assessment.** DOC .github/workflows/ci.yml runs on every push to main and every pull request: pub get -> analyze -> test with a critical-coverage gate -> OSV dependency audit -> secret scan (--require-clean) -> Android host unit tests -> safety mutation kill. DOC .github/workflows/release.yml is tag-triggered and verifies a signed annotated tag, main ancestry and checkout/tag match before building. Flaky test found and root-caused 2026-08-12: test/widgets/panic_button_behavior_test.dart passed in isolation but failed ~50% of full-suite runs. Cause: PanicButton measures the hold with a REAL monotonic Stopwatch (correct -- a wall-clock change must not complete an armed hold), while tester.pump advances only the FakeAsync clock, so the assertion actually depended on machine load. ## 51. Database deployment

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 10


**Section assessment.** SRC: all persistence is on-device - sqflite (local_database_service.dart), flutter_secure_storage (emergency_contacts_v1, user_pin), SharedPreferences (kvkk_consent_log_v2, offline queue). No shared datastore and no tenancy concept exist. There is no operator-run database and therefore no database deployment. The on-device SQLite schema recreates on version upgrade and deliberately holds no authoritative data (contacts live in flutter_secure_storage; see section 29).


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The one genuine local-schema risk - that the recreate-on-upgrade path could someday become destructive - is tracked as the section 29 remediation.

## 52. Backup

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** Backup is deliberately DISABLED and that is the correct security posture here: SRC AndroidManifest.xml sets allowBackup=false and fullBackupContent=false, and android/app/src/main/res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml excludes every domain (root, file, database, sharedpref, external and their device_ counterparts) from both cloud-backup and device-transfer. Under a duress threat model, a cloud copy of a victim's emergency contacts and safety timeline is a liability, not an asset.


**Gap.** There is consequently no user-data recovery path: a lost or wiped device loses the local safety configuration permanently.


**Remediation.** Keep backups disabled. Ensure the onboarding copy sets that expectation - it already says the PIN cannot be reset because there is no server, which is the same message.

SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |

## 53. Disaster Recovery

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 14


**Section assessment.** The only disaster domains that exist are the source repository, the signing key and the Play Console account - not user data (which lives solely on user devices) and not infrastructure (which does not exist).


**Gap.** The catastrophic scenario for this project is loss of the release keystore: DOC per the project's own notes, korubeni_keystore_release.jks is the only keystore with a known password. Losing it means losing the ability to update the app for existing users unless Play App Signing holds the app key.


**Remediation.** Confirm and document whether Play App Signing is enrolled (which makes the upload key replaceable and neutralises this risk entirely). If it is not, enrol it. Record the outcome in docs/release/ - this is the single highest-value DR action available to this project.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

No shared datastore and no tenancy concept exist. There is no central database to lose, corrupt or mass-delete - each device holds only its own owner's data. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |
| `MP-53-002` | Region failure. | N/A | **N/A** | - | SRC: the repository contains no server component - no server routes, no server database, no developer backend. CLAUDE.md rule 1 makes the emergency path backend-free by design; the only outbound calls are OSM map tiles and RevenueCat billing. There is no region, no failover target and no DNS the project operates. Google Play's own availability is Google's concern. | - | Not applicable. | `SRC` |

## 54. Payments varsa

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 32


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/services/revenue_cat_service.dart (entitlement 'KoruBeni Pro') with purchases_flutter/purchases_ui_flutter over Google Play Billing. All money movement, tax, refunds, chargebacks and the billing portal are handled by Google Play - this app never touches a card number. DOC store/BILLING_RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md is the verification script.


**Gap.** The purchase path could not be exercised: a debug build has no RevenueCat key, so RUN reached only the fail-closed entitlement message. No purchase, restore, upgrade, cancel or trial transition has been verified against this build.


**Remediation.** Run DOC store/BILLING_RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md end to end against the Play internal-test track with a licensed test account, and record dated evidence in docs/qa/. This is the largest untested surface in the product and it is the one that takes money.

## 55. Subscription UX

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/constants/feature_access_matrix.dart is a single, well-commented source of truth for the Free/Pro split, with explicit product reasoning (Test Mode is free on purpose because 'paywalling it only forced a trust product to be bought on faith'). RUN confirmed locked Pro features carry a visible lock badge and that free features work unauthenticated.


**Gap.** The paywall screen itself was never reached in RUN because entitlement resolution fails in a debug build, so price, billing period, auto-renewal and cancellation copy are unverified on screen.


**Remediation.** Capture the paywall during the billing-checklist run and verify every disclosure item below against the rendered screen.

## 56. Analytics

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 20


**Section assessment.** SRC lib/core/config/app_environment.dart and lib/core/services/local_logger_service.dart: no Firebase/Crashlytics/Sentry/analytics SDK is present anywhere. Telemetry is deliberately local-only for KVKK compliance. The product ships no analytics SDK, no event pipeline and no funnel instrumentation, deliberately: .claude/rules/common/security.md states 'No developer backend, no analytics, no ad SDKs', and the in-app KVKK text tells users the app collects no advertising or attribution identifier.


**Gap.** The operator is consequently blind to activation, conversion, retention, churn and funnel behaviour. That is a real commercial cost, accepted in exchange for a privacy claim the product makes to users and to Play.


**Remediation.** Do not add an analytics SDK - it would contradict a published KVKK commitment and the Data Safety declaration. Use the aggregate reporting Google Play already provides (installs, uninstalls, subscription retention, crash-free rate), which requires no SDK and no new disclosure, and name an owner for it in the launch runbook.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 57. Experiment / A/B testing

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 10


**Section assessment.** SRC: there is no experimentation framework, no variant assignment and no exposure logging. This follows from having neither a backend nor analytics - a variant could be assigned locally but its effect could never be measured.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. If pricing experiments are ever wanted, Google Play's own store-listing experiments run outside the app and require no SDK.

## 58. SEO — public web ürünüyse

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** SRC: Android-only Flutter app. No ios/ or web/ directory exists; flutter_launcher_icons and flutter_native_splash both set ios:false/web:false. Google Play is the only distribution channel. The only web surface is the GitHub Pages site under .gh-pages-publish/, which exists solely to host the privacy policy and terms at a stable URL for Play Console. It is not a marketing site and is not intended to rank.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The equivalent discipline for this product is App Store Optimisation of the Play listing, which is covered by DOC store/google_play_listing.md, store/play_store_listing_tr.md and TEST test/store_release_docs_test.dart.

## 59. Mobile platform-specific

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 30


**Section assessment.** Android is the only target. Verified: RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). plus the nightly connected-safety matrix across API 29-36 (.github/workflows/nightly-android.yml) and DOC store/ containing the full Play submission pack (data safety, content rating, permissions declaration, listing copy, reviewer access notes).


**Gap.** The 17 iOS requirements do not apply. Several Android requirements are deferred by the deliberate large-screen opt-out, and physical-device evidence for the current build is missing.


**Remediation.** Execute the real-device QA matrix, and treat the large-screen items as the API 37 workstream (section 7).

| `MP-59-026` | Deep/App Links. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED. Deep links exist as of MP-26-008: a CUSTOM SCHEME (korubeni://open), declared in AndroidManifest.xml with a VIEW filter. App Links are deliberately ABSENT rather than deferred -- autoVerify requires /.well-known/assetlinks.json at the DOMAIN ROOT, and this project publishes to a GitHub Pages *project* site whose root belongs to github.io, so the association could never be served. That is a hosting fact, not a difficulty. `scripts/audit_android_release_surface.py` now REFUSES an https launcher scheme and a missing singleTop, so the unprovable claim cannot ship by accident; two cases in `test/android/release_surface_audit_test.dart` prove it fails closed. | None. A custom scheme claims no domain ownership, so the whole surface is provable in this repository. | If the project ever controls a domain root it can serve, an https filter plus a real assetlinks.json would be a separately verifiable change. | `TEST` |
| `MP-59-027` | Battery usage. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P1 | SRC lib/core/services/battery_optimization_service.dart and oem_background_guide_service.dart; RUN confirmed the battery wizard is optional and skippable, with device-specific guidance available. AndroidManifest documents REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS as tied to user-controlled active-session reliability. | Actual battery consumption and timer drift under aggressive OEM battery savers are unmeasured for this build. | Measure on at least one aggressive-OEM physical device and record in docs/audit/ (same as section 41 item 21). | `NONE` |
| `MP-59-028` | Background work rules. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | SRC: the specialUse foreground service, exact alarms (SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM), direct-boot-aware receivers and DeviceProtectedEmergencySessionStore are the sanctioned Android mechanisms for this use case; TEST android/app/src/test/.../DirectBootSafetyKernelTest.kt and AlarmClockRecoveryTest.kt, plus nightly connected instrumentation across API 29-36. | - | - | `RUN` |
| `MP-59-029` | Play policy. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P1 | DOC store/ holds the full Play compliance pack: DATA_SAFETY_FORM.md, google_play_data_safety_answers.md, CONTENT_RATING_ANSWERS.md, permissions_declaration_notes.md, PLAY_CONSOLE_CHECKLIST.md and PLAY_REVIEWER_ACCESS_NOTES.md. SRC AndroidManifest.xml carries per-permission justification comments and deliberately omits USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT with a documented rationale. TEST test/store_release_docs_test.dart enforces docs-to-manifest parity. | CALL_PHONE plus REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS are both sensitive declarations that Play reviews case by case; approval is an external decision that cannot be verified from the repository. | Blocked on Play review. The repository-side preparation is unusually thorough; ensure the declarations submitted match store/permissions_declaration_notes.md exactly. | `DOC` |
| `MP-59-030` | Real device test. | Partially applicable | **PARTIAL** | P1 | CMD `./gradlew :app:connectedPlayDebugAndroidTest` on an arm64 API 36 emulator 2026-08-12 -> BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 4 passed / 0 failed / 2 skipped (reboot probe is script-orchestrated). Passing cases: typedSchedulerAlwaysAcceptsAnInexactBackupOnSupportedDevice, tokenlessLegacyBroadcastCannotDispatch, armIsNeverRefusedForTheOsAlone, theRuntimeSupportsEveryOsThisApkCanBeInstalledOn. | This is emulator evidence. The project's own rules state the Doze race and OEM kill lists cannot be proven off physical hardware. | Execute store/REAL_DEVICE_QA_MATRIX.md on physical devices and record in docs/audit/. Emulator instrumentation now covers the regression surface continuously. | `CMD` |

## 60. UGC / sosyal özellik varsa

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** SRC: the product has no user-generated content, no social feature, no sharing between users, no profiles and no server to host content on. The only user-entered data (an emergency contact's name and number) is private to the device and never transmitted or displayed to anyone else.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The one adjacent obligation - that adding another person's phone number processes their personal data - is handled by the third-party consent dialog verified in RUN (section 34).

## 61. Email

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** SRC: the app sends no email. There is no mail provider, no transactional mail, no server to send from, and TEST test/screens/edit_profile_screen_test.dart proves the email field was deliberately removed from the profile - the product does not even store an address.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The support contact (korubeni.destek@gmail.com) is a published address the user writes to, not a channel the app sends on.

## 62. Legal / compliance

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** This is the most thoroughly evidenced area of the project. SRC lib/constants/legal_texts.dart is the single source for versions and dates, mirrored to store/*.html by scripts/bump_legal.sh. Gates: TEST test/legal_no_first_responder_claims_test.dart, test/public_release_overclaim_copy_test.dart, test/legal_data_export_claims_test.dart, test/store_release_docs_test.dart, test/third_party_notices_test.dart, test/sbom_license_policy_gate_test.dart, test/license_review_queue_test.dart. RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). confirmed the consent gate renders versioned Terms (3.1.0) and KVKK (3.4.0) text with a data-controller identity and contact address.

## 63. Infrastructure / cloud

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 25


**Section assessment.** The project operates no cloud infrastructure. The only 'infrastructure' is GitHub (source, CI, Pages) and the Google Play Console. GitHub-side controls are strong and verifiable: every action pinned to a commit SHA, least-privilege workflow permissions, a production environment for secrets, signed-tag release gating and build attestations (TEST test/release_hardening_contract_test.dart).


**Gap.** Play Console and GitHub account-level settings (MFA, access control, branch protection) live outside the repository and cannot be verified from it.


**Remediation.** Do a one-off account-hygiene pass - confirm MFA on the Google and GitHub accounts, confirm branch protection on main, confirm Play App Signing enrolment - and record the date in docs/release/. That is the whole of this section for this architecture.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 64. Cost / FinOps

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 22


**Section assessment.** Marginal infrastructure cost per user is effectively zero: no servers, no database, no storage, no egress, no logging pipeline and no AI. The only variable third-party costs are the RevenueCat plan tier and Google Play's revenue share; the only externality is OpenStreetMap tile traffic, which is donated infrastructure the project should not abuse.


**Gap.** No cost model or budget alert exists, though the exposure is small and bounded.


**Remediation.** Two concrete actions: confirm the RevenueCat plan tier covers projected subscribers, and estimate OSM tile requests per active user against OSM's bulk-use policy. Skip the rest of this section - it presumes cloud spend this project does not have.

## 65. Support

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 17


**Section assessment.** RUN confirmed Settings exposes 'Yardim & Destek (SSS ve iletisim)', the privacy policy (local and web), and app version information. The published contact address korubeni.destek@gmail.com appears in the in-app KVKK text and in the store material.


**Gap.** Support is a single email address with no ticketing, severity model or response-time commitment - and because there is no telemetry, the operator cannot diagnose a user's problem without the user exporting their own local log.


**Remediation.** Write a one-page support process: how a user exports their local data/log, what to ask for, and a stated response expectation. The export mechanism already exists (lib/core/services/user_data_export_service.dart); the missing piece is telling users and support to use it.

## 66. Internal admin panel

**Applicability:** N/A &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 16


**Section assessment.** SRC: no admin panel, no internal tool and no back office exists - there is no server for one to talk to and no user data reachable off-device. The nearest analogue, the in-app Test Mode, is a user-facing rehearsal feature that dials nothing and is deliberately free.


**Remediation.** Not applicable. The absence of an admin surface removes the entire class of risk this section covers, which is the correct outcome for a duress-model safety product.

## 67. Incident response

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 16


**Section assessment.** DOC docs/veri_ihlali_bildirimi_proseduru.md defines the KVKK data-breach notification procedure, and DOC store/PRODUCTION_ROLLOUT_RUNBOOK.md covers halting a staged rollout.


**Gap.** There is no general incident runbook. For a solo-maintainer offline app most of this section's roles collapse into one person, but the mechanical questions - how to halt a rollout, how to roll forward, how to rotate credentials - should still be written down before they are needed at 3am.


**Remediation.** Write one short incident document answering only the questions that apply here: who notices (Play Console vitals), how to halt a rollout, how to roll forward (not back - Play forbids versionCode regression), how to rotate the RevenueCat key and GitHub secrets, and when the existing breach procedure triggers. Skip incident-commander role assignment and status pages.

The panel reports supportedRefreshRates [60.000004], hasArrSupport false, presentationDeadlineNanos 16666666. The app holds that cadence: mean interval 16.66-16.70ms across every interaction measured (scroll, fling, map pan, route transitions, and the idle SOS breathing animation), with zero intervals at or beyond 2x budget in 744 samples. | None. | Covered by the recorded profile-mode capture. | `RUN` |
| `MP-69-015` | High-refresh screen gerekiyorsa. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | VERIFIED ON A GENUINELY HIGH-REFRESH DISPLAY, not assumed. The AVD was reconfigured to hw.lcd.vsync=120 and rebooted; the panel then reported supportedRefreshRates [120.00001] and SurfaceFlinger peak-refresh-rate 120.00 Hz, confirming the display really was running at 120Hz (the AVD was restored to its 60Hz baseline afterwards). On that panel the app remained correct and smooth: mean interval 16.66-16.67ms, p90 17.3-17.5ms, and ZERO intervals at or beyond 2x budget. BEHAVIOURAL FINDING, recorded rather than glossed: the app presents a stable 60fps and does NOT opt into 120Hz -- the layer's reported period stayed 16.667ms while the display ran at 120Hz. | The app does not request the higher refresh rate. For this product that is a defensible default rather than a defect: the requirement is conditional ('if a high-refresh screen is needed'), a panic-button app gains nothing perceptible from 120fps, and rendering at half the panel rate directly serves the battery budget that sections 41/59 treat as a P1 concern. Recorded so the trade-off is deliberate and revisitable, not accidental. | If a future release wants 120fps, it must be a deliberate change measured against battery drain, not a silent default. | `RUN` |
| `MP-69-016` | Slow CPU. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED UNDER REAL CPU STARVATION, not asserted. The emulator exposes a SINGLE core; four busy-loop processes were spawned to hold it at 100% (top: 100%cpu 100%user 0%idle). Cold launch (`am start -W`, three runs each): baseline 664/688/688 ms, under full contention 3592/3541/3516 ms -- about 5.2x slower. THE APP STILL WORKED: it launched every time with no crash and no ANR, rendered the PIN screen, accepted the PIN and reached Home while the CPU stayed saturated. Frame cost under that load, same instrumentation as section 9: mean 22.88 ms, p90 33.65 ms, worst 44.48 ms, and 16.1% of intervals at or beyond 2x the 16.67 ms budget -- i.e. it genuinely drops frames under starvation. | Dropped frames under CPU saturation are expected and are reported rather than smoothed away; the app degrades in speed but stays functional and reachable, which is what matters for a panic button. A genuinely low-end PHYSICAL handset (slow storage and thermal throttling as well as slow CPU) is still not covered -- that stays with the real-device matrix. | Re-run the recorded contention procedure; remember to kill the busy loops afterwards. | `RUN` |
| `MP-69-017` | Low memory. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | MEASURED on device (API 36 emulator, profile build) with the FOUR lifecycle scenarios kept SEPARATE, because a pause/resume test proves nothing about process death. Memory pressure delivered explicitly at three levels (RUNNING_MODERATE, RUNNING_LOW, RUNNING_CRITICAL) with the app foregrounded: same pid throughout, no crash, screen and in-progress form input intact. Separately, true reclaim was exercised via `am kill` while backgrounded -- the app died and relaunched into the PIN lock screen without losing stored contacts or consent. The distinction matters: a trim-memory callback is NOT process death, and both were run. | Unsubmitted in-progress input does not survive process death -- tracked separately as MP-01-021, not hidden here. | Re-run the recorded matrix. | `RUN` |

## 70. “Kötü kullanıcı” testi

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 25


**Section assessment.** Several adversarial cases are covered by tests rather than by hand: TEST test/widgets/panic_button_behavior_test.dart (rapid press, release before the hold gate), test/services/consent_manager_corrupt_record_test.dart (malformed stored record must not wipe valid consents), android/app/src/test/.../PanicLaunchTest.kt (a third-party app cannot forge a countdown), and the safety mutation suite. RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). exercised invalid input and permission denial.


**Gap.** Hostile-input cases (huge paste, HTML, script, SQL-like strings, unicode garbage, emoji flood) are not exercised anywhere.


**Remediation.** Add one input-robustness test that pushes hostile strings through the contact name and phone fields. The phone field is already constrained by lib/core/utils/emergency_number_validator.dart, so the name field is the real target.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-70-001` | Her butona çok hızlı bas. | Partially applicable | **PASS** | - | R2-02 FIXED. SRC lib/widgets/panic_button.dart guards re-entry with `if (_pointerDown // _isArmed // _countdownOpening) return;` set synchronously in the tap handler, plus a _pressEpoch generation counter. SRC lib/core/widgets/emergency_trigger_host.dart now acquires `_countdownOpen` SYNCHRONOUSLY before the first await and releases it in `finally` -- it used to be written after a ~2.2s entitlement budget, so two quick-access/volume triggers inside that window stacked two countdown routes. ## 71. “Gerçek insan” kalite testi

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** No usability testing with real users is recorded anywhere in the repository. This session's walkthrough is a proxy at best - the operator already knew the product's model.


**Gap.** The checklist asks for five real users given no help. That has not happened, and for a safety product bought on trust it is a material gap: the failure mode is not 'user is confused', it is 'user believes they are protected when they are not'.


**Remediation.** Run an unmoderated first-run test with five people who have never seen the app. Focus on one question above all: after onboarding, can they correctly state what will happen when they press the panic button, and do they know they must rehearse with Test Mode? The product already has the right instinct here (Test Mode is deliberately free precisely so trust does not have to be bought on faith) - this validates whether that lands.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 72. Görsel “premium polish” turu

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 36


**Section assessment.** RUN 2026-08-12: first-run walkthrough on an API 36 emulator (consent gate -> onboarding -> contact gate -> PIN setup -> battery wizard -> home -> settings). Nine full-screen captures were reviewed and no obvious visual defect was found beyond the clipped helper text recorded in section 7. However, no systematic screen-by-screen polish pass was performed, and there is no golden-image suite to catch regressions.


**Gap.** Polish is verified by eye on one device at one density, on a subset of screens. Several items in this list (gradient banding, shadow clipping, optical icon alignment) require deliberate close inspection that was not performed.


**Remediation.** Add Flutter golden tests for the highest-traffic screens - they would both establish the current baseline and catch regressions - then do one deliberate polish pass over the captured goldens.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-72-001` | Pixel misalignment. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | RESOLVED and root cause CORRECTED. The recorded hypothesis (a wrong minHeight on the unlock screen) could not be the source: a ConstrainedBox(minHeight:) inside a SingleChildScrollView receives an UNBOUNDED vertical constraint, so an over-tall child becomes scroll extent and can never raise a RenderFlex overflow -- asserted in test/screens/unlock_screen_density_overflow_test.dart FINDING 1, which is why the earlier negative control kept failing to reproduce. Re-driving the real build at wm density 560 showed the unlock screen clean: full keypad visible, zero overflow in logcat, and 'Sifremi Unuttum' reachable at 170.7 x 64.0 dp. The ACTUAL overflow was then found by stressing density 560 together with font_scale 2.0 and locating Flutter's yellow overflow stripe in a screenshot scan: the Terms version line row had no Expanded and logged 'A RenderFlex overflowed by 82 pixels on the right'. Fixed; re-measured on device as 0 overflow errors under the same stress. | Not cosmetic: PIN entry locks out after 5 failures, so an unreachable backspace turns one mistyped digit into a failed attempt, and the forgot-PIN affordance is unreachable too. A wrong minHeight computation was found and fixed in the same screen, but a negative control FAILED to reproduce the overflow from it, so the offending RenderFlex is a different widget and the root cause is NOT established. | Re-run the recorded reproduction and read the full Flutter error from logcat -- it names the offending widget and its constraints -- then fix that widget and keep the reproduction as a regression test. | `RUN` |

Nine full-screen captures were reviewed and no obvious visual defect was found beyond the clipped helper text recorded in section 7. | `MP-72-030` | Keyboard overlay. | Applicable | **PASS** | - | RE-VERIFIED ON DEVICE 2026-08-13 against this tree, and the previous device claim was WRONG. Driving app-play-debug on arm64 API 36 showed the save action still NOT revealed on a single tap: content ended mid-helper-sentence and both actions were off-screen until a manual scroll. Root cause from a logcat probe: the Android IME animates over ~500ms and didChangeMetrics fires ~15 times; each firing issued a 200ms ensureVisible that the next superseded, all computed against a still-shrinking viewport, settling at offset 107 when ~249 was needed. FIX: a 180ms settle-debounce re-issues the reveal against the FINAL layout. RE-VERIFIED after the fix: both actions fully above the keyboard and the helper text complete. Full write-up with the probe output in docs/audit/device-verification-2026-08-13-r2.md. TEST test/screens/onboarding_contact_step_keyboard_test.dart (8 cases incl. the production PageView embedding and the reserved-padding extent case). | The settle-debounce is DEVICE-verified, not unit-verified: a widget harness steps the IME inset discretely and pumps, so its layout is always settled by the last reveal and it cannot reproduce the defect. This is device-only in the same class as the Doze race. | Keep the device walkthrough in the release checklist; the harness covers the other two mechanisms (scroll reveal, reserved padding) with mutation evidence. | `RUN` |

## 75. Release öncesi engineering gate

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 18


**Section assessment.** CMD `flutter analyze --no-fatal-infos` -> "No issues found!". CMD `flutter test --no-pub` -> 1009 passed / 1 failed; the one failure is the release-change-classification gate flagging the untracked checklist doc, not app code. CMD `dart scripts/verify_critical_coverage.dart` -> CRITICAL_COVERAGE_PASS; all 5 critical safety files >=90% (emergency_session_contract 99.18%, emergency_platform_service 95.04%, pin_verification_service 94.87%, contact_service 93.08%, check_in_service 90.70%). CMD `scripts/audit_dependencies_osv.sh` on a clean worktree -> OSV_EVIDENCE_PASS, pub_queries=197, maven_queries=203, status=PASS, findingCount=0. CMD `scripts/scan_release_secrets.py --require-clean` on a clean worktree -> RELEASE_SECRET_SCAN_PASS, text=706 binary=46 findings=0.


**Gap.** The suite is green apart from one gate failing on the untracked audit documents, and the operational half of this gate (monitoring, alerts, dashboards, on-call) does not exist by architectural choice.


**Remediation.** Classify the new audit documents in config/release_change_classification.json so the release gate returns to green, then decide explicitly which operational items this architecture will simply not have.

## 76. Canary release gate

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 12


**Section assessment.** Google Play staged rollout is the canary mechanism available to this product, and DOC store/PRODUCTION_ROLLOUT_RUNBOOK.md describes using it. No rollout has been performed for this build, so no canary signal exists yet.


**Gap.** Most signals this section asks for cannot be observed at all without telemetry; the ones that can (crash rate, ANR rate, subscription conversion) come from Play Console after a rollout begins.


**Remediation.** Before the first staged rollout, write down which Play Console metrics will be watched, at what threshold the rollout halts, and who checks them - see section 45. Then the canary gate becomes executable rather than aspirational.

## 78. Launch sonrası ilk dönem

**Applicability:** Partially applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 25


**Section assessment.** The product has not launched, so no post-launch observation exists. What is observable after launch is constrained by the deliberate no-telemetry design: Play Console vitals (crash-free rate, ANR rate, excessive wakeups), Play subscription reporting, Play reviews, and user email.


**Gap.** Most items in this section presume telemetry the product will never have.


**Remediation.** Write a short post-launch watch list covering only what is actually observable - Play vitals, subscription retention, store reviews, support volume - with a named owner and a cadence. Explicitly mark the rest as unobservable by design so a future maintainer does not assume the data exists somewhere.

## 79. Her production bug'dan sonra

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 15


**Section assessment.** The project already practises much of this: docs/audit/ and docs/qa/ contain dated investigation records, several past bugs became permanent gates (the flutter_local_notifications v19.0.x release-mode bug became a pinned lower bound with a comment; overclaiming copy became test/public_release_overclaim_copy_test.dart; the PIN lockout integer overflow became a documented cap with a pure testable function), and the safety mutation suite exists to prove the tests actually catch regressions.


**Gap.** The practice is real but informal - there is no postmortem template and no rule that a fixed bug must ship with the test that would have caught it (though .claude/rules/common/testing.md does require new emergency-path behaviour to ship with tests).


**Remediation.** Adopt this checklist section itself as the postmortem template and put it in docs/. The habit already exists; writing it down makes it survive a maintainer change.

## 80. Bir özelliğin “DONE” tanımı

**Applicability:** Applicable &nbsp;·&nbsp; **Items:** 17


**Section assessment.** DOC .claude/rules/common/development-workflow.md defines the project's own definition of done: plan and approval first, small steps each with green tests, emergency-path behaviour shipping with its tests in the same commit, review by the appropriate reviewer agent, then scripts/verify_release.sh if the change touches code, manifest, gradle, legal or CI.


**Gap.** The existing definition covers design intent, implementation, tests, security and release verification well. It does not cover accessibility, motion, analytics, monitoring or rollback because several of those do not exist as capabilities in this architecture.


**Remediation.** Extend the workflow rule with the dimensions that genuinely apply and are currently missing - accessibility assertions and a golden test for any new screen - and record explicitly that analytics and monitoring are not part of done for this product, so their absence reads as a decision rather than an oversight.


| ID | Requirement | Appl. | Status | Sev | Evidence | Gap | Remediation | Verif |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MP-80-001` | all states | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | RUN confirmed rich state coverage in practice (empty, filled, invalid, valid, disabled, success, degraded). | No state matrix is required by the definition of done, and no golden tests enforce it (section 8). | Require a golden test for any new screen. | `DOC` |
| `MP-80-002` | responsive | Applicable | **PARTIAL** | P2 | SRC: portrait phone layout is the supported envelope and it works. | Responsive coverage is deliberately deferred (section 7). | Blocked on the API 37 adaptive-layout workstream. | `DOC` |
